One of the early commentators takes the reason of the telling in the other sense, which is that the account is the encouragement for the leniency with the people who plotted, and the leniency is the example of the brother who did not raise his hand against the brother who threatened, and he cites the report that God gives the community the example of the two sons of Adam and tells them to take the path of the better and leave the path of the worse.

**The Two Offerings**

The account states the two offerings of the two sons, and the two are the two sons of Adam, whose names in the reports are Qābil and Hābil. The exegetes divide over the occasion of the offerings, and the division is the one the account allows. In the first account, the two were commanded to offer the sacrifice, and the offering was the act of the command. In the second account, the two offered in the competition for the right to the marriage of the twin sister, whom both desired, and the offering was the act of the competition. The two are the herdsman and the grower, and the herdsman offered the fattened sheep and the grower offered the produce of the crop, and the account states that the offering of the one was accepted and the offering of the other was not, and like the account of the scripture the sūrah confirms, the sūrah’s account does not state the reason for the choice. Some of the commentators give the reason in the form of the report, which is that the one offered his most prized sheep and the other offered the poorest of the produce. The word for the offering carries the root that is the root of the nearness, and the offering is understood primarily as the means of the drawing near to God, and the nearness is the mark of the offering that is accepted.

**The Threat and the Answer**

The account states the threat of the one whose offering was not accepted, and the threat is stated in the single sentence that the sūrah keeps. The brother who was not accepted says to the brother who was accepted that he will kill him, and the statement is the act by which the envy becomes the threat, and the threat is the form the envy takes when it is stated to the one who is the object of it. The answer of the brother is the answer that the account states in the closing words of the verse, which are the words that state the reason of the acceptance. God accepts only from the one who is reverent, and the reverent is the one who fears God in the act of the offering, and the answer is the answer that names the ground of the acceptance, which is the ground that the offering of the other did not have. The threat and the answer are the two words that the account states between the offering and the killing, and the two words are the form of the story that the sūrah keeps for the reader.

**The Sacrifice as the Form of the Act**

One of the early commentators states the relation of the sacrifice of the earlier communities to the acts of the community of the sūrah, and the relation is the one that the account allows. The practice of the sacrificial offering of the earlier religions is like the practice of the charity and the alms of the Muslims, and the acts of the religion, the prayer and the fasting and the mandatory charity, take the place of the sacrifice in the form of the new religion. The verb that is used for the offering is the verb that is used for the giving of the charity, and the use is the mark of the relation. The rites of the pilgrimage end with the slaughter of the animals for the sacrifice, and the feast of the sacrifice commemorates the act of God who halted the test of the father who was commanded to offer the son and provided the ram in the place of the son, and the meat of the slaughter is given in the charity. The verse that states the sacrifice of the pilgrimage is the verse that names the piety as the thing that reaches God, and the statement is the statement that the offering is the act that the piety gives its form, which is to say the sacrifice of the account is the form of the act that the reverent does, and the reverent is the one the verse names as the one from whom the offering is accepted.

**A Story That Is a Prototype**

The account of the two sons is the account the sūrah tells in the form that the scripture it confirms tells, and the form is the form of the first killing in the story of the book of the community that received the account. The account serves in the sūrah as the prototype of the inclination of the fallen human being to the violence and the killing of the one who is his brother, and the prototype is the form the sūrah uses when it tells the moral danger in the story that the community knows. The story is read in the context of the sūrah as the subtle commentary on the matters of the People of the Book that the sūrah raises in its account of the two communities, and the reading is the reading that the context of the sūrah supports, since the sūrah is the sūrah of the covenant and the breaking, and the account of the two sons is the account of the first breaking in the story of the human being, which is the breaking of the brother against the brother in the moment of the envy. The prototype is the story the community is meant to hear as the story of its own possibility, and the possibility is the possibility the sūrah states in the verses that follow the account.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:28
> If you raise your hand to kill me, I will not raise mine to kill you, because I fear Allah—the Lord of all worlds.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Refusal That Answers the Threat**

The verse states the answer of the brother to the threat of the previous verse, and the answer is the refusal of the violence in the form that the refusal takes. The one who is threatened says to the one who threatens that he will not raise his hand to kill, and the statement is the act by which the threat is met with the refusal, and the refusal is the form of the answer that the account keeps. The question the exegetes take up is the question that the answer raises, which is the question of the one who refuses the self-defense in the moment of the threat that is stated, and the question is the question the account is meant to answer in the form the answer gives. The verse states the refusal and the ground of the refusal together, and the two together are the form of the answer that the sūrah keeps for the reader.

**The Grounds the Exegetes Give**

The exegetes give the grounds for the refusal, and the grounds are the several that the account allows. In the first ground, the refusal is the act by which the enormity of the intention of the brother is clarified and emphasized, and the refusal is the act that shows the threat in the form the threat is, since the one who threatens is shown in the act of the one who does not answer. In the second ground, the attack in the self-defense had not been made lawful at that stage of the story of the human being, and the refusal is the act that the state of the law did not permit. In the third ground, the statement is not the abandonment of the self-defense but the abandonment of the intention to kill the brother before the killing or in the process of the defense, and the refusal is the refusal of the preemptive killing, which is to say the one who refuses is the one who does not take the life before the life is taken. In the fourth ground, the accounts that state the killing in the sleep of the one who was killed show that the one who was killed was not in the state to defend himself, and the state does not preclude the defense had the one been awake. In the fifth ground, the one who refused considered it better to be killed than to kill, even in the self-defense, and the consideration is the act of the one who puts the life of the brother above the life in the form the life is kept.

**The Fear That Is the Ground**

The closing words of the verse state the ground of the refusal, and the ground is the fear of God in the form the verse names. The one who refuses says that he fears God, the Lord of the worlds, and the fear is the taqwā that the sūrah uses throughout for the state of the one who is set apart by the reverence. The name that follows the fear is the name that states the scope of the fear, which is the Lord of the worlds, and the scope is the scope that the fear of the one who refuses takes, since the fear is not the fear of the judge of the two brothers but the fear of the Lord of all the worlds, and the Lord of all the worlds is the one who sees the act in the place the act is done and in the place the act is not done. The fear is the ground that the refusal stands on, and the ground is the ground that the verse states in the closing words, which is to say the refusal is not the act of the weakness but the act of the one who stands on the ground that the verse names.

**The Example the Early Muslims Cited**

The exegetes preserve the account of the way the early Muslims took the example of the brother who refused, and the account is the one that states the use of the example in the moment of the community’s own test. The pious and the prominent among the early Muslims, among them the man whose name the reports give, abstained from the fighting of the fellow Muslims and the taking of the sides in the first division of the community in the years after the death of the Prophet, and they cited the example of the brother who refused as the ground of the abstention. The abstention is the act of the one who demonstrates the strength in the leaving of the violence that could end in the death of the one who is a fellow of the faith, even if the one is the one who has disobeyed in the moment, and the citing is the act by which the community takes the example of the account for its own moment. The example is the example the sūrah keeps for the community that faces the moment of the violence against the fellow of the faith, and the moment is the moment the community can be in.

**The Word That Stands Against the Hand**

The verse states the answer in the form that the answer takes in the account, and the form is the form of the word against the hand. The threat of the previous verse is the hand that is stretched in the statement, and the answer of this verse is the hand that is not stretched, and the two are the two hands that the account keeps in the two verses. The structure of the two verses is the structure of the story at its turning point, and the turning point is the moment where the threat is met and the meeting is the meeting that the account is told to show. The sūrah states the two verses in the order that the story takes, and the order is the form of the account, which is to state the threat and then to state the answer, and to let the reader take the measure of the two from the two words, since the words are the act in the form the act is kept in the story.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:29
> I want to let you bear your sin against me along with your other sins, then you will be one of those destined to the Fire. And that is the reward of the wrongdoers.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Preference That Is Stated**

The verse states the closing words of the brother who refused the violence, and the words are the statement of the preference that the refusal carries. Rather than the attack in return, the one who is threatened prefers that the brother take the full burden of the sin, and the preference is stated in the form of the wish that the verse keeps. The preference is the act that the refusal completes, since the refusal of the previous verse is the act of the hand that is not raised and this verse is the act of the word that states the burden, and the two together are the form of the answer that the account gives. The verse states the preference and the consequence of the preference together, and the two together are the closing of the words of the one who is killed, which is to say the sūrah keeps the last words of the brother in the form that the words are, and the form is the form of the warning that the one who is killed puts in the mouth of the story.

**The Two Sins That Are Named**

The verse names the two sins that the one who is killed puts on the brother, and the naming is the naming that the grammar of the verse states. The exegetes take up the first of the two, which is the sin that is named in the singular as the sin of the one who speaks, and they state that the sin is not the sin that the brother would bear in the place of the one who speaks, since the Book and the tradition of the Book are clear that no one takes the burden of the sin of the other, and the verse that states the principle is the verse that names the fact that no soul burdened with the sin bears the burden of the other (6:164). The sin that is named is the sin of the killing of the one who speaks, and the word for the killing is the word that the grammar leaves out, which is to say the sin is the sin of the killing and the killing is the act that is named in the ellipsis. The second of the two sins is the sin of the brother in the other acts of the disobedience that the brother has done before the moment of the threat, and the two together are the full burden that the one who is killed puts on the one who kills.

**The Burden That Is the Warning**

The preference that the verse states is the warning that the verse keeps, and the warning is the statement of the burden that the killing puts on the one who kills. The one who is killed puts the burden in the form of the wish, and the wish is the form the warning takes in the mouth of the one who is killed, since the one who is killed is the one who states the burden that the killing carries, and the stating is the act that the story keeps for the reader. The burden is the burden of the two sins together, which is the sin of the killing and the sin of the disobedience that came before, and the two together are the state of the one who is destined to the Fire, which is the state the verse names in the closing words. The warning is not the threat of the one who is killed; it is the statement of the fact that the killing puts on the one who kills, and the fact is the fact the sūrah states in the words of the story.

**The Reward of the Wrongdoers**

The closing words of the verse are the formula that states the measure of the state, and the formula is the one the Book uses for the reward of the one who is the wrongdoer. The reward is the measure that the state of the one who is destined to the Fire takes, and the measure is the measure that the verse names in the form of the closing. The formula appears in the Book as the standing statement of the consequence of the wrongdoing, and the appearance here, at the end of the words of the one who is killed, is the appearance that the account gives the formula, which is to say the story closes the words of the brother with the statement of the measure, and the measure is the measure that the story states for the reader. The verse ends the words of the one who is killed, and the ending is the end of the account of the words, since the account of the act that follows is the account that the verse after tells in the form of the killing.

**The Words That End the Answer**

The two verses of the answer, the refusal and the warning, are the two parts of the words of the one who is killed, and the two parts together are the form of the answer that the sūrah keeps. The refusal is the act of the hand that is not raised, and the warning is the act of the word that states the burden, and the two are the two acts of the one who is killed in the moment of the threat. The sūrah states the two in the two verses, and the stating is the method of the account, which is to keep the words of the one who is killed in the form that the words are, and to let the reader take the measure of the answer from the two verses. The account of the two sons is the account that the sūrah tells in the form that the scripture it confirms tells, and the form is the form that keeps the words of the one who is killed as the words that are the answer to the threat, and the answer is the answer that the story is told to show.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:30
> Yet Cain convinced himself to kill his brother, so he killed him—becoming a loser.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Soul That Incites the Act**

The verse states the act that the account has been building to, and the act is stated in the form that names the thing that did the act. The soul of the one who kills is the thing that incited him to the killing, and the inciting is the act by which the intention becomes the act. The exegetes state the several senses of the inciting, and the senses are the ones that the word carries. The soul led the one who kills to the killing, and it made the killing easy for him, and it made the killing seem good to him, and it emboldened him to the act of the crime, and the four are the four forms of the inciting that the word states. The soul in the verse is the soul that is the seat of the passion that drives the human being to the act that is not the act of the reason, and the seat is the sense that the Book uses for the soul in the verses that state the inclination of the soul to the evil, the verse where the prophet states that he does not free himself from the blame and that the soul is ever inclined to the evil except those on whom God has mercy (12:53).

**The Intention That Was Not Checked**

One of the commentators states the account of the state of the soul in the moment after the intention is formed, and the account is the one that explains the silence of the answer that the previous verses stated. Once the soul, under the influence of the empty desires, has formed the intention of the evil, the calls to the right and the just act are muted in the ear of the one whose soul has formed the intention, and the muting is the state of the one who has taken the intention. The account is the account that the commentator gives for the fact that the answer of the brother, the refusal and the warning, had no effect in the moment, and the no effect is the state of the soul that has formed the intention, which is to say the words of the one who is killed reached the one who kills in the state where the words do not reach, and the state is the state the verse states in the form of the inciting.

**The Killing and Its Manner**

The verse states the killing in the single clause that the account keeps, and the manner of the killing is the matter of the reports that the exegetes preserve. In the majority of the reports, the one who kills slew his brother in the sleep of the brother, and the act was the crushing of the head between the two stones, and the idea of the act is the one that some of the reports attribute to the whisper of the one who is the tempter. The state of the sleep is the state that the account of the previous verse allows, since the one who was killed was not in the state to defend himself, and the defense that the refusal did not abandon was the defense that the state did not give. The reports add the fact of the father in the years after the killing, and the fact is that the father did not laugh again in the years that were left to him, and the fact is the state of the grief that the killing put on the one who received the killing as the act of his son.

**The Loss That Is Stated**

The verse closes with the statement of the loss, and the loss is the state that the act puts the one who acts in. The one who kills becomes the one who is the loser, and the becoming is the act by which the killing changes the state of the one who kills from the state of the one who has the offering to the state of the one who has the loss. The loss is the loss that the warning of the previous verse stated, which is the loss of the one who is destined to the Fire, and the stating of the loss in this verse is the fulfillment of the warning in the form of the act. The sūrah states the loss in the closing words of the verse, and the closing is the end of the account of the act, since the account of the consequence of the act is the account that the verses after tell in the form of the crow and the law of the life for the life. The loss is the state that the story states for the one who kills, and the state is the state the story is told to show.

**The Story That Is Kept in the Form**

The account of the two sons is kept in the sūrah in the form that the account takes from the offering to the loss, and the form is the form of the prototype that the sūrah uses for the story of the first killing. The account states the offering and the acceptance and the threat and the answer and the inciting and the killing and the loss, and the seven are the parts of the story that the sūrah keeps in the verses that run from the command to relate to the statement of the loss. The keeping of the story in the form is the method of the teaching, which is to tell the story of the first killing in the form that the community that hears it can take as the story of the killing that it can do, and the taking is the act that the sūrah expects of the reader, since the story is the story of the human being and not the story of the one man who lived in the time of the father. The verse states the loss, and the loss is the end of the story in the form the story is kept, and the end is the end that the sūrah gives to the account of the two sons.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:31
> Then Allah sent a crow digging ˹a grave in the ground ˹for a dead crow˺, in order to show him how to bury the corpse of his brother. He cried, “Alas! Have I ˹even˺ failed to be like this crow and bury the corpse of my brother?” So he became regretful.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The First Death and the Ignorance That Follows**

The verse states the act that the story needs after the killing, and the act is the act of the one who does not know how to deal with the dead body of the one he has killed. Some of the exegetes state that the one who was killed was the first of the human beings to be killed, and some of the exegetes go further and state that he was the first to die, and the state that follows the fact is the state of the ignorance. The one who kills does not know how to conceal the body and protect it from the beasts, and the not knowing is the fact that the rite of the burial had not been established in the time of the act. The story keeps the ignorance in the account, and the keeping is the way the sūrah states the state of the human being in the moment after the first killing, since the ignorance is the state that the killing puts the one who kills in, and the state is the state the story is told to show.

**The Crow That Teaches the Rite**

The verse states the act of God in the moment of the ignorance, and the act is the sending of the crow. The crow is the living bird that scratches the ground with the beak and the feet, and the scratching is the act by which the bird buries the other bird, and the burial is the rite that the act shows to the one who is watching. The rite of the burial is, on that account, the rite that God taught to the human being, and the teaching is the act that the verse states in the form of the sending. The other accounts that the exegetes preserve state the act of the crow in the other forms, which is that the bird was digging in the ground to bury another thing and the digging gave the one who was watching the idea of the burial, and that the bird came and began to cover the body of the dead with the earth and the covering gave the one who was watching the sight of the dignity that the victim was given. The two accounts are the two forms of the act that the verse states, and the act is the act of the teaching that the story keeps.

**The Word That Carries the Shame**

The verse uses a word for the state of the body that the one who kills sees, and the word carries the double sense that the exegetes note. The word can be used for the private parts of the one who is exposed, and it can be used for the state of the shame that the exposure puts on the one who is seen. One of the commentators takes the word in the second sense, and he states that the word denotes the shame of the body that is exposed in the ground, and the shame is the state that the one who kills sees and does not cover. The other commentator takes the word in the first sense, and he states that the word is the word for the body in the state of the exposure, and the body is the thing that the one who kills does not know how to deal with. The two senses are the two forms of the word, and the word is the word the verse keeps for the state that the story shows.

**The Cry That Names the Regret**

The verse states the words of the one who was watching in the form of the cry, and the cry is the exclamation that the Book uses for the spiritual regret in the moment when the certainty of the punishment is realized. The cry appears in the Book in the verses that state the state of the one who sees the account, and the state is the state of the one who takes the measure of his own act in the light of the act that he has seen. The one who was watching takes the measure of his own state in the light of the act of the bird, and the measure is the measure that the cry states, since the cry is the act by which the one who sees the act of the bird sees the act that he did not do for the one who was his brother. The cry is the act of the recognition, and the recognition is the act that the story keeps for the reader.

**The Remorse That Is the State**

The verse closes with the statement of the state that the cry produces, and the state is the state of the one who is the regretful. The becoming is the act by which the one who kills is put in the state that the story states for him, and the state is the state that the account keeps for the one who is the object of the teaching. The story reaches the moral centre in this verse, and the centre is the moment where the one who kills sees the act that he did not do and takes the measure of the state that he is in. The sūrah states the remorse in the closing words of the verse, and the closing is the end of the account of the story in the form of the story, since the account that follows the verse is the account of the law that the story produces, and the law is the law that the verse after states for the community that receives the account.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:32
> That is why We ordained for the Children of Israel that whoever takes a life—unless as a punishment for murder or mischief in the land—it will be as if they killed all of humanity; and whoever saves a life, it will be as if they saved all of humanity. ˹Although˺ Our messengers already came to them with clear proofs, many of them still transgressed afterwards through the land.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Law That the Story Produces**

The verse opens with the conjunction that connects the law to the story, and the conjunction is the way the sūrah states that the account of the two sons is the ground of the ordination. The law is ordained for the Children of Israel, and the ordination is the act by which the gravity of the killing is stated in the form of the law. The account of the story and the account of the scripture it confirms are focused on the gravity and the horror of the act, and the two accounts are the ground of the law that the verse states. The difficulty of concealing the crime of the killing, which the story shows in the form of the body that is not buried and the scripture shows in the form of the blood that is said to cry from the ground, is the fact that the law states as the likelihood of the punishment in this world and in the next. The verse is the verse that closes the account, and the closing is the act by which the story is turned into the law.

**The Equivalence of the One and the All**

The law states the equivalence in the two directions, and the equivalence is the statement that the killing of the one is like the killing of all, and the saving of the one is like the saving of all. The exegetes divide over the sense of the first of the two, and the division is the one that the statement allows. In the first sense, the soul that is killed is the soul of the particular spiritual importance, the prophet or the just one who is the imam, and the killing of that soul is the killing of all in the measure of the importance. In the second sense, the killing of the one is the killing of all from the point of view of the one who is killed, and the all is the all that the one who is killed represents in the measure of the loss. In the third sense, the gravity is taken from the point of view of the offense against the Creator, and the offense is the violation of what God has made most sacred, whether the one who kills kills the one or kills the many. The tradition of the commentary of the community that received the account states the same connection, and the connection is the one that became the part of the ethics of the community in the books that carry the tradition.

**The Exception That Is Stated**

The law states the exception in the middle of the statement, and the exception is the killing that is the act of the lawful punishment. The exception exempts the killing in the course of the carrying out of the capital punishment against the one who has killed another, and the killing for the other crime that carries the penalty of the capital punishment, and the crime is the crime that the exegetes name. The second part of the exception is the killing of the one who works the mischief in the land, and the mischief is, in the reading the exegetes give, the idolatry, the waging of the war against the community, and the terrorizing of the roads, and the meaning of the mischief in this verse is the meaning that the verse after states in the form of the law of the one who makes the war. The exception is stated in the law so that the law is the law that the community can apply, and the applying is the act that the law is meant to permit.

**The Saving of the Life**

The second statement of the equivalence is the statement of the saving, and the saving is the act that the law names in the form of the saving of the one. The exegetes take the saving in the several senses that the word allows. In the first sense, the saving is the act of the kin of the one who is killed who forgo the retaliation and take the compensation, or who forgive the one who killed without the compensation, and the forgiving is the act that the verse of the law of the retaliation states in the form of the concession. In the second sense, the saving is the act of the one who saves the person from the peril that takes the life, the fire and the drowning, and the saving is the act that the danger does not permit to be delayed. In the third sense, the saving is the act that is more indirect and more spiritual, the lifting of the one out of the poverty and the giving of the guidance to the one who is astray, and the broadening is the act that the word of the saving allows.

**Why the Community Is Named**

The law is stated for the Children of Israel in the form of the ordination, and the naming of the community is the matter the exegetes take up. In the first account, the community is named because it is the first religious community that is threatened with the eternal punishment for the sin of the killing, and the threat is the fact that the ordination follows. In the second account, the community is named because of the plot of the certain ones of the community against the Prophet, which the sūrah states in the account of the hands that were stretched, and the naming is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the address. In the third account, the principle that the law states is the principle that applies to the community of the sūrah as it applies to the community that is named, and the applying is the act that the law is meant to do. The verse closes with the statement of the transgression, and the transgression is the fact that the messengers came with the clear proofs and many of the community were prodigal in the land after the coming, and the fact is the fact the sūrah states for the community it addresses.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:33
> Indeed, the penalty for those who wage war against Allah and His Messenger and spread mischief in the land is death, crucifixion, cutting off their hands and feet on opposite sides, or exile from the land. This ˹penalty˺ is a disgrace for them in this world, and they will suffer a tremendous punishment in the Hereafter.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Law of the One Who Makes the War**

The verse states the penalty for the one who wages the war against God and His Messenger and works the mischief in the land, and the penalty is the one of the five punishments that the Book ordains for the crimes that undermine the fabric of the community. The phrase that names the one who makes the war is the phrase that the exegetes understand in the sense that the phrase allows, which is that the one who makes the war is the one who attacks and terrorizes the members of the community directly, since the one cannot make the war against God in the form the phrase states. The crimes that the penalty covers are the crimes that are named in the term that the law uses, and the crimes are the armed robbery, the assault, and the murder of the innocent on the road, and the crimes are the crimes that the use of the weapon and the threat of the violence distinguishes from the other forms of the taking and the assault, since the instilling of the fear is the mark of the crime that the law names.

**The Meaning That the Exegetes Fix**

The phrase for the working of the mischief appears open to the wide reading, and the meaning of the phrase in this verse is the meaning that the majority of the commentators fix in the armed crimes and their effect on the safety of the community. The phrase appears in the Book in the other places, and in the other places it is connected with the wickedness in the land and opposed to the setting aright of the things, and it is used in the places that name the spiritual corruption and its consequence in the next world. The verse where the angels foresee the placing of the human being on the earth states the fact that the one who is placed will work the corruption and shed the blood (2:30), and the linking is the link that connects the working of the corruption to the physical violence, and the link is the fact that the story of the two sons that the sūrah has just told shows in the form of the story. The verse states the penalty for the one who works the mischief, and the penalty is the penalty that the law gives to the act that the story shows in the form of the first killing.

**The Occasion That the Reports Preserve**

The reports that the exegetes preserve give the occasion of the verse in the incident that the sūrah tells in the form of the account. A group of men from two of the tribes came to the Prophet in the form of the desire to embrace the religion, and the Prophet accepted them into the community, and the men complained that the settled life of the city was the life they could not bear, and the Prophet allowed them to leave and sent with them the keeper of the camels and the camels for the milk. Outside the city, the men maimed and killed the keeper and took the camels, and the Prophet had the men seized and gave the order for the penalties that the reports state. The verse came down after the order, and the verse established the penalty for the crimes in the form of the four that the verse lists, and the list is the list that the verse of the account of the prophecy of the one who threatened with the cutting of the hands and the feet on the opposite sides and the crucifixion states in the form of the threat (20:71).

**The Question of the Severity**

The exegetes take up the question of the relation of the verse to the order that the reports state, and the question is the one the account raises. The men were the ones who had embraced the religion in the presence of the Prophet and renounced it by the act, and a small minority of the exegetes takes the verse as the verse that applies to the ones who leave the religion in the general form. The majority of the accounts hold that the severe penalties of the verse pertain to the ones who commit the crimes in the form of the brazen act and the exceptional brutality and the terrorizing of the innocent. Some of the exegetes take the verse as the clarification that comes in the light of the order, and some take it as the partial criticism and the abrogation of the severity, since the verse does not contain the act that the order named beyond the four that it lists. Some read the verse as the implicit banning of the torture, and the fact that the Prophet refrained from the torture before and after the incident is the fact that supports the reading. The other accounts hold that the Prophet desisted from the act when the verse came down, or that the act beyond the four was the act of the retaliation for the maiming that the men had done, and the retaliation is the law the Book states in the verse of the retaliation.

**The Four Penalties and Their Measure**

Some of the commentators and a minority of the legal authorities hold that the authority of the state can use the discretion in the applying of the one of the four penalties or the combination of them for the one who is convicted, and the holding is the holding that the law allows. The other of the legal scholars holds that the penalties correlate to the measure of the crime in the specific ways, and the ways are the ones the law states. The one who kills in the course of the crime and does not take is the one who is executed, and the one who takes and does not kill is the one whose hand and foot are cut on the opposite sides, and the one who does both is the one who is killed and exposed, and the one who terrifies and does neither is the one who is banished. The crucifixion, in the understanding that is usual in the history of the community, is the exposure of the criminal in the public, and it is carried out after the execution as the means of the deterrence, and some of the scholars hold that the exposure is before the execution and not the means of it, so that the one who is exposed is killed after the exposure and does not endure the suffering that is the torture that is forbidden. The banishment is, in the reading of the majority, the exile from the land of the one to another land or the imprisonment that is the banishment from the society, and the imprisonment is the punishment that the second of the caliphs is reported to have instituted first, keeping the one who is convicted until the repentance is the full repentance. The verse closes with the statement of the disgrace in this world and the tremendous punishment in the next, and the two are the two parts of the penalty that the law states.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:34
> As for those who repent before you seize them, then know that Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Door That Is Left Open**

The verse opens with the exception that the verse before does not state, and the exception is the door of the repentance that is left open for the one who has done the crimes that the penalty of the previous verse names. The door is the door that the Book leaves open for all the sins, even the grave ones, and the opening is the act that the sūrah states in the form of the exception at the head of the verse. The repentance for the crimes that the previous verse names may spare the one who repents the punishment in the next world, and the sparing in this world is the sparing that the verse states in the form of the condition, since the condition is the moment that the verse names for the repentance that spares the worldly punishment. The structure of the two verses is the structure of the sūrah’s method, which is to state the severity of the law and then to state the door that the law keeps, and the two together are the form of the law that the sūrah gives.

**The Moment That Decides**

The condition of the verse is the moment, and the moment is the one that is before the seizure. The one who repents is the one who repents before he is seized and brought to the authority, and the moment is the moment the verse names for the sparing of the worldly punishment. The parallel that the verse carries is the parallel of the repentance that spares the punishment in the next world only when it is made before the death, after which the sentence of God is certain, and the parallel is the one the Book states in the verses that name the repentance before the death and the repentance that is not accepted after the death begins. In most of the schools of the law, the penalty that is ordained is the penalty that is applied when the one who does the grave crime is brought before the authority and is convicted, and the applying is the act that the law does after the seizure. The repentance before the seizure is the repentance that the verse names, and the repentance after the seizure is the repentance that the law does not name as the act that spares the penalty.

**The Rights That the Repentance Does Not Take**

The verse states the sparing of the penalty, and the sparing is the sparing that the law does in the form of the repentance. The rights of the victims are the rights that the repentance does not take, since the one who repents is still the one who is required to pay the restitution for the property that is damaged or taken, and the one who is liable to the retaliation for the life that is taken. The retaliation is the law that the Book states in the verse of the law of the retaliation, and the law is the law that the rights of the victims keep in the form of the right of the kin of the one who is killed. The victim has the right to the justice that is exacted from the one who does the crime, and the victim has the right to the forgiveness that avoids the public denunciation, and the forgiveness is the act that the verse names in the closing words, since the forgiveness is the act that is of the higher spiritual merit and the act that is the participation in the attributes that the closing of the verse names.

**The Closing That Names the Attribute**

The verse closes with the statement of the two attributes, and the attributes are the All-Forgiving and the Most Merciful. The closing is the act by which the sūrah states the ground of the door that the verse keeps, since the door of the repentance is the door that the attribute of the forgiveness opens, and the attribute is the attribute the Giver has in the form that the Giver gives it. The structure of the two verses of the law is the structure that the sūrah keeps for the law of the grave crime, which is to state the penalty in the first and to state the door in the second, and to close the door with the name of the one who opens it. The verse states the repentance and the attribute together, and the two together are the form of the law that the sūrah gives, which is the form of the severity that keeps the mercy and the mercy that keeps the severity, and the two together are the law that the community is meant to apply in the form the sūrah states.

**The Form the Sūrah Keeps for the Law**

The structure of the two verses, the penalty and the door, is the form the sūrah keeps for the law of the grave act, and the form is the form the sūrah uses in the other laws it states. The law of the thief that the sūrah states in the verse of the hand that is cut is closed with the statement of the one who repents after the wrongdoing and mends the way, and the sūrah turns to him in the forgiveness, and the closing is the same closing that this verse keeps. The form is the method of the sūrah in the law, which is to state the severity in the first place and to state the mercy in the second, and to let the two stand together in the account so that the community that receives the law receives the law in the form the law is, which is the form of the severity that keeps the door and the door that keeps the severity. The community that applies the law in the form the sūrah states is the community that applies the penalty when the penalty is the act and that keeps the door when the door is the state, and the applying of the two together is the act that the form requires.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:35
> O believers! Be mindful of Allah and seek what brings you closer to Him and struggle in His Way, so you may be successful.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Command After the Law**

The verse opens the address to the believers in the moment after the two verses of the law, and the address is the act that the sūrah does after it has stated the penalty and the door. The verse states the three commands, and the three are the fear of God, the seeking of the means of the nearness, and the striving in the way, and the three together are the form of the command that the sūrah gives to the community that has heard the law. The position of the verse is the position that gives it the function, since the community that has heard the penalty and the door is the community that is now told the path that is the path of the one who keeps the law and takes the door when the door is the need. The command is the command that the sūrah states for the community in the form of the address, and the address is the act that the sūrah does at the turning point of the account.

**The Means of the Nearness**

The second command of the verse is the command to seek the means that bring the one near to God, and the nearness is the concept that the Book states in the two forms. In the first form, the nearness is the high spiritual goal and the reward of the one who seeks it, and the verses that state the goal are the verses that name the nearness as the thing the one who is the object of the call seeks. In the second form, the nearness is the result of the acts of the obedience and the virtue, and the verses that state the result are the verses that name the near ones as the ones who do the acts. The ones who hold the highest rank in the state of the next world are the ones who are brought near, and the bringing near is the act that the Book states for the ones who do the acts of the obedience. The command of the verse, in the context of the two verses of the law, is the command that alludes to the act of the repentance of the one who sins and the act of the acceptance of the repentance by the one who is the victim, and the two are the two forms of the means that the context of the sūrah gives. The ones who seek the nearness by the other means, the means of the intercession of the false gods, are the ones the Book rebukes, and the rebuke is the statement that the devotion is due to God alone and that the ones who take the others as the means are the ones who are judged in the matter they differ about. The sacred tradition that the reports preserve states the best of the means in the form of the words of God, and the words are the statement that the servant does not draw near to God with anything more beloved to Him than the acts that are ordained, and that the servant continues to draw near by the acts that are the voluntary acts until God loves him, and the report is carried in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (6502).

**The Striving in the Way**

The third command of the verse is the command to strive in the way of God, and the striving is the command that the Book states in the verses that name the act of the one who believes and does the act in the cause of God. The striving is the act that the command of the nearness completes, since the nearness is the state that the striving produces and the striving is the act that the nearness is meant to direct. The verses that state the striving are the verses that name the rank of the one who strives with the wealth and the life in the cause of God, and the rank is the rank that the striving gives in the sight of God. The command of the verse is the command that the sūrah states for the community in the form of the address, and the community is the community that has heard the law and the door, and the striving is the act that the community is meant to do in the place where the law is the law and the door is the door.

**The Three Commands as the One Path**

The three commands of the verse are the three parts of the one path, and the path is the path that the sūrah states for the community after the law. The fear of God is the state that the path requires, and the seeking of the means of the nearness is the act that the state produces, and the striving in the way is the act that the nearness directs. The success that the verse names as the end of the three is the success that the path gives, and the success is the state of the one who walks the path in the form the path is walked. The sūrah states the three commands in the single verse, and the stating is the method of the address, which is to give the community the path in the form of the command, and the command is the command that the community is meant to take in the moment after the law, since the moment after the law is the moment where the community can take the law as the law and the door as the door, and the path is the path that keeps the two together in the one act of the obedience.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:36
> As for the disbelievers, even if they were to possess everything in the world twice over ˹and offer it all˺ to ransom themselves from the punishment of the Day of Judgment, it would never be accepted from them. And they will suffer a painful punishment.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Ransom That Is Refused**

The verse states the condition of the one who disbelieves in the moment of the account, and the condition is stated in the form of the ransom that is refused. The one who disbelieves is put in the state where the possession is the thing that can be offered, and the offering is the act that the state permits, and the refusing is the act that the verse states for the one who receives the offering. The ransom is the image that the verse uses for the wealth of the one who disbelieves in the moment of the judgment, and the image is the image that the state of the one who disbelieves takes in the hour when the wealth is the thing that is put forward and the wealth is not the thing that is accepted. The verse states the refusal in the form of the certainty, and the certainty is the act that the verse does in the statement of the condition.

**The Measure of the Possession**

The verse states the measure of the possession in the form that the measure takes, and the measure is everything that is on the earth and the like of it besides. The doubling is the act that the verse does in the statement of the wealth, since the wealth of the one who disbelieves is not the wealth that is on the earth alone but the wealth that is on the earth and the wealth that is equal to it, and the two together are the measure that the verse states for the possession. The measure is the measure that the state of the one who disbelieves takes in the moment of the judgment, which is to say the one who disbelieves is put in the state where the possession is the full possession and the full possession is not the possession that is accepted. The refusal of the acceptance is the act that the verse states against the measure, and the act is the act that the verse does in the statement of the condition of the one who disbelieves.

**The Painful Punishment**

The verse closes with the statement of the punishment, and the punishment is the painful punishment that the verse names. The naming of the punishment is the act that the verse does at the end of the statement of the condition, and the end is the end that the sūrah gives to the verse of the ransom. The painful punishment is the state that the one who disbelieves is put in when the ransom is refused, and the state is the state that the verse after describes in the form of the desire to get out and the not getting out, and the two verses together are the account of the state of the one who disbelieves in the moment of the judgment. The sūrah states the punishment in the closing words of the verse, and the closing is the end of the statement of the condition, since the statement of the condition is the statement that the sūrah gives to the community it addresses in the form of the warning.

**The Verse in the Flow of the Account**

The position of the verse in the sūrah is the position that gives it the function in the account. The verse comes after the address of the command that the sūrah stated for the community in the verse before, and the address is the address of the path, and the verse of the ransom is the verse that states the state of the one who does not take the path. The two are the two sides of the account that the sūrah keeps, the side of the one who takes the path and the side of the one who does not take it, and the two sides together are the form of the account that the sūrah gives at this point of the narrative. The community that hears the verse is the community that is meant to take the verse as the statement of the state that is the state of the one who does not take the path, and the taking is the act that the warning does in the form of the statement of the condition.

**The Two Forms of the End**

The sūrah states, in the verses that run from the law of the grave act to this verse, the two forms of the end that the community can take. In the first form, the one who has done the act takes the door of the repentance before the seizure, and the repentance is the act that the attribute of the forgiveness receives, and the end of the one who takes the door is the end that the attribute names. In the second form, the one who does not take the path in the life takes the state of the one who disbelieves in the moment of the judgment, and the state is the state where the wealth is the thing that is offered and the wealth is not the thing that is accepted. The two forms are the two ends that the sūrah keeps in the account, and the keeping is the method of the warning, which is to show the community the two ends in the form of the account and to let the community take its end in the life rather than in the moment of the judgment. The verse states the second of the two, and the stating is the act that the warning does at the point of the account where the path has been stated and the state of the one who does not take it is the state that the community is meant to see.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:37
> They will be desperate to get out of the Fire but they will never be able to. And they will suffer an everlasting punishment.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Wish and the Not Getting Out**

The verse states the state of the one who is in the Fire in the form of the wish that is not the act. The one who is in the Fire is the one who desires to come out, and the desire is the act that the state produces, and the not coming out is the fact that the verse states against the desire. The two are the two parts of the state that the verse keeps, the part of the wish and the part of the certainty, and the two together are the form of the state that the sūrah gives to the one who is the object of the account. The verse does not state the act of the trying; it states the state of the one who is in the place where the trying is the act that the state does not permit to be the act that gets out, and the not getting out is the fact that the verse states in the form of the certainty.

**The Series of the Refused Ransom**

The verse belongs to the series of the Book’s statements of the futility of the ransom with the goods of this world in the moment of the judgment, and the series is the series that the certainty of the account requires. The verses that state the futility are the verses where no ransom is taken and no intercession is accepted and no help is given, and the series runs through the Book in the verses that name the day of the account, including the verse that states that no ransom is taken in the day when no soul is of help to another (2:48, 2:123), and the verse that states that the disbeliever who dies in the disbelief would offer the gold that fills the world and the offering would not be accepted (3:91), and the verse that states that the wrongdoer who has everything in the world would ransom himself with it and would not be ransomed (10:54), and the verse that states the same fact in the measure of the twice over (13:18), and the verse that states it in the form of the address to the hypocrites and the disbelievers (57:15). The verse of this sūrah belongs to the series, and the belonging is the fact that the sūrah states the futility in the form of the state of the one who is in the Fire, which is the form of the futility that the series states in the several forms.

**The Lasting as the State**

The verse closes with the statement of the punishment in the form of the lasting, and the lasting is the state that the punishment takes in the verse. The lasting is the state that contrasts with the state of the trial of this world, and the trial is the trial that the community endures in the life and the trial is not the state that the verse names. The state that the verse names is the state of the one who is in the Fire in the time that does not end in the coming out, and the not ending is the fact that the verse states in the form of the everlasting. The verse of the sūrah that states the state of the residents of the Fire in the opening of the account of the disbelievers is the verse that this verse completes, and the two together are the statement of the state in the form of the residence and the statement of the state in the form of the wish that is not the act.

**The Pair as the Complete Account**

The two verses that meet in this account, the verse of the ransom and the verse of the wish, are the two parts of the complete statement of the state of the one who disbelieves in the moment of the judgment. The first states the refusal of the acceptance of the wealth that is offered, and the second states the desire of the one who is in the place and the certainty of the one who does not get out, and the two together are the account of the state in the form that the state is. The sūrah keeps the two verses in the order that the account takes, and the order is the order of the state, which is the order of the refusal and then the order of the wish, since the wish is the act that follows the refusal, and the one who is refused the ransom is the one who wishes to come out and does not come out. The pair is the form that the sūrah gives to the account of the state, and the account is the account that the community is meant to hear as the account of the end that is the end of the one who does not take the path.

**A Verse at the Point of the Account**

The position of the verse in the sūrah is the position that gives it the function in the flow of the account. The verse comes after the address of the command that the sūrah stated for the community, and after the verse of the ransom that states the state of the one who does not take the path, and the verse of the wish is the verse that completes the statement of the state at the point of the account. The sūrah states the state of the one who does not take the path in the two verses, and the stating is the act that the warning does in the form of the account, and the account is the account that the community that hears the sūrah is meant to take as the account of the end, since the end is the end that the sūrah states for the one who does not take the path in the form of the state that the two verses keep.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:38
> As for male and female thieves, cut off their hands for what they have done—a deterrent from Allah. And Allah is Almighty, All-Wise.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Law of the Taking**

The verse states the penalty for the one who takes, and the penalty is the one of the five punishments that the Book ordains for the crimes that have the effect on the society at large. The amputation of the hand is the punishment for the theft, and the taking is the crime that the law names in the form of the act of the one who takes what is not the thing that is given to him. One of the commentators notes that the amputation was the punishment for the taking in the period before the religion in the land of the Arabs, and the note is the fact that the law of the verse states in the form of the penalty that the custom of the land knew, and the stating is the act by which the law of the religion takes the custom and puts it in the form of the command that is the command of God.

**The Measure That the Law Requires**

The exegetes and the legal authorities take up the question of the measure of the taking that the penalty applies to, and the question is the one the verse raises in the form of the law. Some of the commentators held that the penalty applies to all the forms of the taking without the distinction of the amount or the degree, and the majority of the legal authorities held, on the ground of the report of the Prophet and the precedent of the early caliphs, that the penalty applies to the taking of the significant sum or the very precious object. The minimum for which the penalty is applied is given in the several measures, which is three dirhams in the silver, and the most common of the measures is the quarter of the dīnār in the gold, and the equivalent of the two in the goods, and some of the measures put the minimum at the worth of the shield. Below the measure, the penalty is the restitution or the compensation for the goods that are taken, and the restitution is the act that one of the legal scholars considers to be the act that is required even for the one to whom the penalty of the punishment is also applied.

**The Equality That Is Stated**

The verse states the penalty for the male and the female in the same breath, and the stating of the two together is the act by which the verse makes the equality the fact of the law. The equality is the form that the law takes in the verse of the law of the fornication, where the male and the female are the two who receive the same penalty, and the equality is the form that the law keeps for the punishments that are the punishments of the Book. The report that the exegetes preserve from the occasion of the state of the equality is the report of the one who came to the Prophet on behalf of the woman of the taking, and the Prophet stated that the nations before were destroyed when the noble of them took and they left him and the weak of them took and they did the punishment on him, and he stated by the one in whose hand is the soul that if the daughter of the Prophet were to take, he would cut off the hand, and the report is carried in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (6788). The report is the report that the equality of the law takes in the form of the word of the one who is the object of the law.

**The Penalty That Disables and Does Not Kill**

The penalty that the verse states is the penalty that is meant to be the disabling, and the disabling is the act that the penalty does in the form of the act that does not take the life. The law of the community requires the act of the medical procedure that stops the bleeding and prevents the death, and the requiring is the act that the law does in the form of the care for the one who is the object of the penalty. The punishments that are the punishments of the Book are understood as the right that God has against the one who does the crime that has the effect of the destabilizing on the society, and the right is the right that the penalty serves in the two forms, which is the form of the recompense for the crime and the form of the exemplary punishment that is meant to deter the others and keep the incidence of the crime in the measure that is the low measure. The verse closes with the statement of the two attributes, and the attributes are the Almighty and the All-Wise, and the closing is the act by which the sūrah states the ground of the penalty in the form of the power that does the act and the wisdom that puts the act in the measure that the act requires.

**The Two Laws That Share the Hand**

The sūrah has already named the cutting off of the hand and the foot on the opposite sides in the law of the one who makes the war, and the law of this verse is the law that names the cutting off of the hand alone for the taking. The two laws share the act of the hand, and the sharing is the fact that the law keeps for the two crimes in the different measures. The crime of the war is the crime that takes the life and the property together in the act of the terror, and the crime of the taking is the crime that takes the property in the act that is not the act of the terror, and the two are the two measures that the law of the sūrah keeps in the two verses. The community that applies the two laws is the community that applies the law in the form that the law gives to each of the two, and the applying is the act that the measure requires, since the measure of the crime is the measure that the penalty takes in the form the verse states.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:39
> But whoever repents after their wrongdoing and mends their ways, Allah will surely turn to them in forgiveness. Indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Door After the Hand**

The verse opens with the conjunction that keeps the door after the law of the hand, and the door is the door of the repentance that the sūrah states for the one who has done the taking. The form of the two verses, the penalty and the door, is the form that the sūrah keeps for the law of the grave act, and the keeping is the method of the sūrah in the law, which is to state the severity in the first place and to state the mercy in the second, and to let the two stand together in the account so that the community receives the law in the form the law is. The one who repents is the one who repents after the wrongdoing and makes the amendment, and the amendment is the act that the repentance requires, since the repentance is the act of the heart and the amendment is the act of the way, and the two together are the form of the returning that the verse states.

**The Debate on the Sparing**

The commentators take up the question of whether the repentance spares the one who takes from the penalty of the amputation, and the question is the one the two verses raise. Some of the commentators held that the repentance spares the one who takes from the penalty, and the holding is the holding that the door of the verse opens in the full form. The others held that the repentance does not spare the penalty in this world, and that the relenting of God toward the one who repents, which the verse promises, pertains to the judgment and the punishment in the next world, and the holding is the holding that keeps the penalty in the form of the act that the law does after the wrongdoing. The two holdings are the two readings of the door, and the door is the door that the sūrah states in the form that the law allows, which is the form of the mercy that the law keeps without the taking of the form of the law.

**The Purification of the Punishment**

The reports that the exegetes preserve state the act of the Prophet toward the one who takes and repents after the penalty is done, and the act is the act that the reports give in the form of the assurance. Several of the reports indicate that the Prophet assured the ones who took and repented and had undergone the penalty that they were forgiven by God and that they were purified of the sin by the act of the punishment, and the assurance is the act that the reports state for the one who is the object of the penalty and the object of the repentance. The assurance is the act that the state of the one who is the object of the two takes in the form of the word of the one who is the prophet, and the word is the word that the sūrah’s law is meant to give to the community in the moment of the penalty and the repentance.

**The Forgiveness That Averts the Penalty**

The foundational scholars of the law, among them the two whose names the reports give first, held that the penalty is the act that is averted only when the one who is the object of the taking forgives the one who takes and does not bring the matter to the authority, and the holding is the holding that the law states in the form of the right of the victim. The holding is the same holding that the law states in the case of the crimes of the armed offense that the sūrah told in the verses before, and the sameness is the fact that the law keeps for the two cases in the form of the right that the victim has over the act of the denunciation. The community that applies the law in the form the scholars state is the community that keeps the right of the victim in the form of the right, and the keeping is the act that the law does in the form of the choice that the victim is given.

**The Closing That Names the Attribute**

The verse closes with the statement of the two attributes, and the attributes are the All-Forgiving and the Most Merciful. The closing is the act by which the sūrah states the ground of the door that the verse keeps, since the door of the repentance is the door that the attribute of the forgiveness opens, and the attribute is the attribute that the Giver has in the form that the Giver gives it. The structure of the two verses of the law of the taking is the structure that the sūrah keeps for the law of the grave act, and the keeping is the method of the sūrah, which is to close the law of the severity with the name of the one who opens the door of the mercy. The verse states the repentance and the attribute together, and the two together are the form of the law that the sūrah gives, which is the form of the penalty that keeps the door and the door that keeps the penalty, and the community is meant to take the two in the form the sūrah states.

**The Two Parts of the Return**

The verse names the two acts that make up the return, and the two acts are the repentance and the amendment. The repentance is the act of the heart that turns from the wrongdoing, and the amendment is the act of the way that the turning produces in the life of the one who turns, and the two together are the form of the return that the verse states. The one who repents is the one who does the two, and the one who does the one without the other is the one whose return is not the return that the verse names, since the return is the act of the heart and the act of the way together, and the two are the two parts of the one act. The sūrah states the two in the single clause of the verse, and the stating is the method of the command, which is to give the community the return in the form that the return is, and the form is the form of the turning that is the turning in the heart and in the way at the same act.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:40
> Do you not know that the kingdom of the heavens and the earth belongs to Allah ˹alone˺? He punishes whoever He wills and forgives whoever He wills. And Allah is Most Capable of everything.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Closing of the Section of the Penalty**

The verse closes the section of the sūrah that runs from the law of the one who makes the war to the door of the repentance of the one who takes, and the closing is the act by which the sūrah states the ground of the section in the form of the reminder. The section is the section of the penalty and the door, and the ground that the verse states is the ground of the one who is the judge over the two, which is the form of God as the ultimate judge and the form of the Giver of the two acts, the punishing and the forgiving. The verse is the verse that the sūrah puts at the end of the section, and the putting is the act by which the section is closed in the form that the section is meant to be closed, since the section is the section of the law and the law is the law of the one who has the kingdom of the heavens and the earth.

**The Kingdom as the Ground**

The verse opens with the question that states the kingdom, and the kingdom is the word that carries the double sense that the commentators note. The word means the sovereignty and it means the ownership, and the two senses are the two forms of the state that the one who has the kingdom holds over the creation. One of the commentators takes the verse in the form of the support of the thesis that the state of God gives him the inherent right to punish some and forgive others, even when the state may be the state that is not the state the human mind takes as the state of the measure, and the support is the support that the kingdom gives, since the kingdom is the state of the Creator over the created, and the Creator is the one who has the right over the created in the form that the created does not have the right over the created. The thesis is the thesis of the school that the commentator represents, and the support is the act that the verse does in the form of the statement of the kingdom.

**The Pair of the Acts**

The verse states the two acts of the one who has the kingdom, and the two acts are the punishing and the forgiving. The pair is the pair that the sūrah states in the verse where the claim of the sonship is met with the statement of the kingdom, and the stating of the pair in this verse is the stating that the sūrah does at the close of the section of the penalty, which is to state the two acts in the form that the two acts are the acts of the one who has the kingdom over the heavens and the earth. The two acts are the acts that the section of the sūrah states in the several forms, and the forms are the forms of the penalty that is the act of the punishing and the forms of the door that is the act of the forgiving, and the two together are the section that the verse closes in the form of the statement of the two acts.

**A Verse Without the Case**

The form of the verse is the form that distinguishes it from the verses of the section that it closes, and the distinction is the fact that the verse does not state the case, and the case is the case of the community or the case of the crime or the case of the story. The verse states the principle in the form of the principle, and the principle is the statement of the kingdom and the statement of the two acts and the statement of the capability over all things, and the three together are the form of the verse that the sūrah gives at the point of the account where the section of the law ends and the address of the prophet begins. The verse is the hinge of the account, and the hinge is the act by which the sūrah turns from the section of the law to the address that follows, and the turning is the act that the verse does in the form of the statement of the ground that is the ground of both the section and the address.

**The Capability That Closes the Statement**

The verse closes with the statement of the capability over all things, and the statement is the statement that the sūrah uses as the closing of the several of its verses. The first verse of the sūrah closes with the statement that God commands what He wills, and the verses of the address to the People of the Book close with the statement of the capability, and the pair of the verses of the claim of the divinity and the claim of the sonship close with the statement of the kingdom and the capability together. The closing is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the sovereignty, and the sovereignty is the ground that the sūrah states for the law and for the address in the same form, since the law is the law of the one who has the kingdom and the address is the address of the one who has the capability over all things. The verse of this section closes with the statement of the capability, and the closing is the act that the sūrah does at the end of the section of the penalty, which is to put the section under the ground that is the ground of the sūrah.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:41
> O Messenger! Do not grieve for those who race to disbelieve—those who say, “We believe” with their tongues, but their hearts are in disbelief. Nor those among the Jews who eagerly listen to lies, attentive to those who are too arrogant to come to you. They distort the Scripture, taking rulings out of context, then say, “If this is the ruling you get ˹from Muḥammad˺, accept it. If not, beware!” Whoever Allah allows to be deluded, you can never be of any help to them against Allah. It is not Allah’s Will to purify their hearts. For them there is disgrace in this world, and they will suffer a tremendous punishment in the Hereafter.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Comfort of the Address**

The verse opens with the command that is the form of the comfort, and the comfort is the act that the sūrah does for the Prophet in the moment of the rejection of the message. The command is the command not to grieve for the ones who hasten to the disbelief, and the grief is the state that the Prophet is put in by the act of the rejection, and the not grieving is the act that the sūrah commands in the form of the address. The ones who are the objects of the address are the ones in the two groups, the group of the ones who say the word of the belief with the tongue and the heart does not believe, and the group of the ones of the community of the scripture who listen to the lie. Some of the commentators hold that the two groups are not the two who are put together in the statement, and the holding is the holding that the reading of the verse allows, since the verse can be read in the form that separates the two groups in the statement of the condition. The verse belongs to the series of the Book’s addresses of the comfort to the Prophet in the moment of the rejection, and the series is the series that the sūrah states in the several verses that name the act of the one who is the object of the rejection.

**The Lie and the Listening**

The verse states the act of the group of the ones who listen, and the act is the act of the listening to the lie and the listening to the ones who do not come to the Prophet. The listening to the lie is understood in the two senses that the commentators give, which is the sense of the listening to the lie and the taking of the lie as the truth, and the sense of the listening to the prophet in the form of the listening that is the act of the lying or the denying of what is said. The ones who do not come to the Prophet are the ones of the community of the scripture who are outside the city, and the reports name the place in the town that is the place of the ones who give the instruction. The instruction that the reports state is the instruction that the verse keeps in the form of the conditional, which is the statement that if the ruling that is the ruling of the first of the two is given, the one who is the object of the instruction takes it, and if the ruling that is the second of the two is given, the one who is the object of the instruction is the one who is warned.

**The Case That the Reports Give**
