The reports that the exegetes preserve give the case of the matters that the verse states, and the case is the case of the two of the community of the scripture who were brought for the judgment in the matter of the fornication. The two were from the town outside the city, and the community of the city brought them to the Prophet on behalf of the ones of their own community outside. The Prophet asked about the ruling of the scripture in the matter, and the information that was given was the information that was not the information of the scripture, since the ones who gave the information sought to hide the ruling of the stoning and to avert the punishment from the two. The accounts state that the ones of the community brought the Prophet to the young one of their scholars, and the young one confirmed the ruling of the stoning in the scripture, and the accounts add that the leaders of the community had decided to change the ruling when they became the ones who were not the ones to carry it out against the ones of the high standing, and they put the other ruling of the beating and the public shame in the place of the stoning. The Prophet decided on the stoning after the confirmation, and the deciding is the act that the accounts state as the act of the revival of the original teaching of the scripture.

**The Distortion in the Form of the Words**

The verse states the act of the distortion in the form that the distortion takes in the word, and the form is the form of the moving of the word from the place of the word. The word of the distortion can mean the act of the moving of the words from the proper place in the text, and some of the accounts take the act of the leaders of the community as the act of the removing of the passage of the stoning from the place of the passage in the discussion of the fornication in the scripture. The act of the distortion is the act that the Book states in the several verses that name the act of the ones of the community of the scripture, and the stating is the stating that the sūrah does in the form of the charge that the verse keeps. The distortion is the act that the hardened heart does in the form of the act of the hand, and the two are the two forms of the state that the sūrah states for the one who is the object of the charge.

**The Power That Is Not Given**

The verse states the act of the one who is deluded in the form of the act that is not the act of the prophet, and the act is the act of the delusion that God wills for the one who is the object of the delusion. The word that is used for the delusion is the word that carries the two senses of the trial and the temptation, and in the negative context of the verse the majority of the commentators take the sense of the putting in the error, and the taking is the taking that the state of the one who is put in the error requires. When God wills that the one is in the error, no one, and not even the prophet, has the power to change the spiritual state of the one against the will of God, and the not having of the power is the act that the verse states in the form of the comfort, since the prophet is not the one who is the object of the grief when the state of the one who is deluded is the state that the prophet does not have the power to change. The verse closes with the statement of the disgrace in this world and the tremendous punishment in the next, and the two are the two parts of the state that the verse states for the one who is the object of the address.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:42
> They eagerly listen to falsehood and consume forbidden gain. So if they come to you ˹O Prophet˺, either judge between them or turn away from them. If you turn away from them, they cannot harm you whatsoever. But if you judge between them, then do so with justice. Surely Allah loves those who are just.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Gain That Is Consumed**

The verse continues the account of the group that the previous verse addressed, and the account is the account of the act of the listening to the false and the consuming of the unlawful. The consuming of the unlawful in this verse is not the consuming of the food, and the sense is the sense of the taking of the gain that is not the gain that is lawful, and the taking is, in the reading that the exegetes give, the taking of the bribe in the specific form. The act of the consuming is the act that the sūrah states for the group in the several verses, and the stating is the stating that the verse does in the form of the charge that follows the charge of the listening, since the listening to the false and the taking of the unlawful are the two acts of the one who is the object of the account.

**The Option of the Judge**

The verse states the option that the law gives to the one who is the judge, and the option is the option of the judging between the ones who come or the turning away from them. The incident and the verse are the ground on which the scholars of the law take the question of the right of the authority of the state to judge in the matters of the ones who are not of the religion within the state of the religion, and the taking up is the taking up that the law does in the form of the question of the grave matter between the two of the ones who are not of the religion. Two of the scholars held that the ruler may choose to judge the case or to abstain from it, and the choosing is the act that the law gives to the ruler in the form of the option, and the abstaining is the act by which the case is judged within the community of the religion of the ones who are the objects of the case. Some of the scholars held that the option was the act that the verse of the command to judge by what is revealed abrogated, and the others did not hold the abrogation, and the two holdings are the two readings of the relation of the two verses in the law. The scholars considered it the duty of the ruler to judge in the grave disputes of the ones who are not of the religion when the one of the religion is involved in the dispute, and the judging is the act that the law does in the form of the duty when the matter is brought to the ruler, since the matter is judged according to the law of the religion when it is the matter that is brought.

**The Justice That Is the Condition**

The verse states the condition of the judging in the form of the command, and the command is the command to judge with the justice. The justice is the condition that the law requires of the one who judges, and the requiring is the act that the Book does in the several verses that name the act of the judging, including the verse that commands the returning of the trusts and the judging with the fairness in the matter between the people (4:58), and the verse that commands the standing firm for the justice as the witness for God even when the witness is against the self and the kin (4:135). The two verses are the two statements of the condition that the verse of this sūrah states in the form of the command to the one who is the judge, and the command is the command that the sūrah gives to the prophet in the form of the option that is the option of the judging, since the judging is the act that the justice is meant to direct.

**The Closing That Names the Attribute**

The verse closes with the statement of the love of the one who is just, and the love is the act that the attribute of the one who loves does in the form of the statement of the one who is the object of the love. The formula is the formula that the Book states in the verse of the address to the believers in the matter of the dealing kindly and fairly with the ones who do not fight and do not drive out, and the stating of the formula in this verse is the stating that the sūrah does at the close of the account of the group that the previous verses addressed, which is to close the account with the name of the attribute that the act of the justice participates in. The justice is the act of the one who is the object of the love, and the love is the act that the attribute does in the form of the statement of the one who is just, and the two together are the form of the closing that the sūrah keeps for the account of the judging.

**The Harm That Is Stated as Not Harm**

The verse states, in the middle of the option, the statement of the harm that is not the harm, and the statement is the act that the sūrah does for the prophet in the form of the comfort. If the prophet turns away from the ones who come, the ones do not have the power to harm him in the least, and the not having of the power is the act that the verse states in the form of the certainty. The state of the judge who turns away is the state of the one who keeps the power of the judgment in the form of the power, and the turning away is not the act that takes the power from the judge, since the power is the power of the one who is the judge and not the power of the ones who come. The verse states the two options and the statement of the not harm together, and the three are the form of the law that the sūrah gives to the one who is the judge, which is the form of the option that the judge has and the power that the option does not take.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:43
> But why do they come to you for judgment when they ˹already˺ have the Torah containing Allah’s judgment, then they turn away after all? They are not ˹true˺ believers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Question That Names the Inconsistency**

The verse opens with the question that is the form of the charge, and the charge is the charge of the inconsistency between the act and the possession. The community that comes to the prophet for the judgment is the community that has the Torah, and the Torah is the book that contains the judgment of God, so the coming is the act that the possession does not require. The exegetes take the question in the sense that the question allows, which is the sense of the insincerity of the coming, since the community that comes is the community that seeks the ruling that is the more lenient of the two, and the seeking is the act that the coming does in the form of the request. The question is not the question of the ignorance; it is the question of the one who knows and comes in the form of the one who does not know, and the form is the form the sūrah keeps for the act of the coming.

**The Scripture That Stands in the Presence**

The verse, with the verses that follow it to the verse of the Book that confirms, states the fact that the Torah and the Gospel remain the sources of the judgment and the guidance for the two communities, and the fact is stated even in the presence of the prophet. The sūrah states the fact again at the close of the account, where the address to the People of the Book is the command to observe the Torah, the Gospel, and the revelation of the Lord (5:68), and the two statements together are the sūrah’s position on the standing of the two scriptures, which is the standing of the sources that the community can judge by and the guidance that the community can follow. The coming to the prophet is not the act that the standing of the scripture requires, since the scripture is the book that contains the judgment, and the judgment is the act the scripture gives to the one who judges.

**The Turning That Is the Fact**

The verse states the act that follows the coming, and the act is the turning away after the judgment is the thing they have. The turning away is the act that the community does in the moment when the judgment is available to them, and the turning is the act that the sūrah names as the fact of the state, since the state of the one who turns away from the judgment he has is the state that the verse states in the form of the charge. The closing words of the verse put the turning in the form of the state of the belief, and the state is the state of the one who is not the believer in the form the belief requires, since the belief is the act of the one who takes the judgment he has and judges by it, and the one who turns away from the judgment is the one who does not take the act of the belief in the form the belief is.

**A Question for the Community That Hears**

The sūrah puts the question in the account so that the community that hears the sūrah takes the question as the question of its own state, and the taking is the act that the sūrah expects of the reader. The community that hears is the community that has the Book and the judgment, and the question is the question of the one who has the Book and the judgment and comes to the other for the ruling that is the lenient of the two. The sūrah states the question in the form that the account requires, and the form is the form of the address that the sūrah keeps for the community it tells, since the account of the coming and the turning is the account that the community is meant to hear as the account of the state it can be in when the judgment is the thing it has and the coming is the act it does.

**The Judgment That Is Available and Not Lost**

The question of the verse rests on the fact that the judgment is available to the community that comes, and the availability is the fact that the sūrah states against the act of the coming. The Torah is not the book whose judgment is lost or whose ruling is not to be found; it is the book that contains the judgment of God in the form that the judgment is to be applied, and the containing is the act that the verse states in the phrase that names the book. The community that comes to the prophet is the community that could have judged by the book and did not judge by the book, and the could have is the act that the sūrah puts in the form of the question, since the question is the question of the one who has the means and does not take the means. The sūrah states the availability of the judgment in the verse of the account, and the stating is the act that the sūrah does so that the community that hears takes the availability as the fact of its own state, which is the fact that the judgment of the book is the thing that stands in the presence of the community that holds the book, and the presence is the presence that the coming to the other does not remove.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:44
> Indeed, We revealed the Torah, containing guidance and light, by which the prophets, who submitted themselves to Allah, made judgments for Jews. So too did the rabbis and scholars judge according to Allah’s Book, with which they were entrusted and of which they were made keepers. So do not fear the people; fear Me! Nor trade my revelations for a fleeting gain. And those who do not judge by what Allah has revealed are ˹truly˺ the disbelievers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Guidance and Light in the Book of Moses**

The verse states the revelation of the Torah in the form that the Book uses for the scripture, and the form is the form of the guidance and the light. The Torah is described in this verse as the book that contains the guidance and the light, and the description is the description the Book gives to the book of Moses in the verse where the one who splits the book and hides much of it is met with the question of who revealed the book that came through Moses as the light and the guidance for the people (6:91). The two descriptions are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that the scripture is the book that guides and the book that gives the light, and the two together are the form the sūrah keeps for the Torah in the account of the judgment.

**The Judges the Verse Names**

The verse names the three who judge by the book, and the three are the prophets, the sages, and the scholars. The prophets who submitted to God are, in the reading the exegetes give most weight, the prophets of the people who lived between Moses and Jesus, and the reading is the one that the context of the verse supports, since the verse is the verse of the judgment in the community between the two. A minority of the readings takes the phrase in the sense of the submission and connects it to the prophet of the sūrah, and the connection is the one the word allows but the context does not require. The word for the sages is the word the early commentators define in the several ways, which is the way of the legal and the political authority among the community, the way of the ascetic devotee among the descendants of the priestly line, and the way of the most authoritative scholar who is qualified to issue the decisive ruling in the law, and the last of the three is the way that compares them to the jurist of the community of the sūrah. The word for the scholars is the word that is glossed as the religious scholar of the community, and the gloss is the gloss the commentators give without the division.

**The Two Warnings of the Verse**

The verse states the two warnings, and the two are the warning against the fear of the people and the warning against the trading of the signs. The first is the command that the sūrah states in the verse of the perfection of the religion and in the verses of the other sūrahs that name the same command, and the command is the command to the one who judges, since the judge who fears the people is the judge who does not judge by the book in the form the book requires. The second is the metaphorical warning that the Book uses against the one who takes the weighty matter of the religion and gives it in the exchange for the fleeting gain of the world, and the warning is the warning the Book states in the several verses that name the act of the selling of the revelation and the covenant for the gain, and the two warnings together are the act by which the sūrah commands the authority and the scholar of the community to keep the book in the form it was revealed, without the altering that the fear of the people produces and without the altering that the gain of the standing produces.

**The One Who Does Not Judge**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the one who does not judge by what God revealed, and the state is the state of the disbeliever in the sense that the word carries beyond the sense of the disbelief in the absolute form. The word can mean the one who covers and hides, and the meaning is the meaning that the incident the sūrah tells gives to the state, since the community of the incident is the community that sought to conceal the ruling of the book rather than the community that is in the state of the disbelief of the faith. The statement is repeated in the two verses that follow, and the repetition is the repetition that states the two other names of the state, the name of the wrongdoer in the verse of the retribution and the name of the iniquitous in the verse of the Gospel. The early commentator is reported to have put the distinction in the form of the act, which is that the one who opposes and rejects the judgment of God is the disbeliever, and the one who simply fails to judge by the law is the wrongdoer and the iniquitous, and the distinction is the distinction that the three verses keep in the three names.

**The Book as the Trust of the Keepers**

The verse states the relation of the book to the scholars in the form of the trust, and the trust is the act by which the book is put in the hands of the one who is the keeper. The book is the book with which they were entrusted, and the entrusting is the act that God does in the revelation of the book to the community, and the keeping is the act that the community is to do in the form of the guarding of the book in the state it was revealed. The verse names the keepers as the witnesses of the book, and the witnessing is the act that the keeping produces, since the one who keeps the book is the one who testifies to the book in the judgment he gives by it. The trust and the witnessing are the two forms of the state that the verse states for the scholar of the community, and the two together are the ground of the two warnings that the verse states, since the scholar who fears the people is the scholar who does not keep the trust in the form the trust requires, and the scholar who trades the signs is the scholar who does not keep the witnessing in the form the witnessing requires.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:45
> We ordained for them in the Torah, “A life for a life, an eye for an eye, a nose for a nose, an ear for an ear, a tooth for a tooth—and for wounds equal retaliation.” But whoever waives it charitably, it will be atonement for them. And those who do not judge by what Allah has revealed are ˹truly˺ the wrongdoers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Law the Scripture Carries**

The verse states the law of the retribution in the form that the scripture of Moses carries, and the form is the form of the equivalence, which is the life for the life, the eye for the eye, the nose for the nose, the ear for the ear, the tooth for the tooth, and the equal retaliation for the wounds. The account of the scripture that the sūrah confirms carries the same law in the passages of the book of the law, and the carrying is the fact that the verse of this sūrah states in the form of the description. The verse, in the reading of one of the commentators, is the verse that reminds the community of the law of the retribution for the killing and the injury, and the reminding is the act that the sūrah does in the account of the neglect, since some of the accounts hold that the verses of the account refer not only to the matter of the penalty of the fornication but also to the matter of the neglect of the retribution in the killing.

**The Standing That the Law Removes**

The accounts that the exegetes preserve state the act of the two clans of the community in the matter of the retribution, and the act is the act of the altering of the required retribution in accordance with the relative standing of the one who kills and the one who is killed. The clan that is of the higher standing is the clan whose member who kills the member of the lower clan does not face the retribution but pays the compensation, and the reverse is the situation where the member of the lower clan who kills the member of the higher clan is killed or pays the higher amount. The verse of the sūrah states the true ruling of the scripture in the matter, and the ruling is the ruling that makes no accommodation for the standing of the two and makes no provision for the payment of the compensation as the alternative, which is the alternative the law of the community of the sūrah allows. The law of the scripture, in the statement of the verse, allows the equivalent retribution or the outright forgiveness, and the two are the two forms the law keeps without the third form of the adjustment.

**The Atonement of the One Who Forgives**

The verse states the act of the one who forgoes the retribution in the form of the charity, and the act is the act that the verse names as the atonement. The commentators differ over the one for whom the atonement is the atonement, and the difference is the difference of the two readings. In the first reading, the atonement is the atonement of the sins of the one who forgives, as the reward of the act of the charity, and the reading is the one that one of the commentators argues for, since the report of the tradition states that the atonement of the one who is the offender comes through the carrying out of the penalty, and the commentator cites the report that states that the forgoing of the right of the retribution in the act of the charity serves as the atonement of the sins of the one who forgives. In the second reading, the atonement is the atonement of the sin of the one who is forgiven, and the reading is the one that the verse of the reward of the one who pardons and seeks the reconciliation suggests, since the verse states the reward of God for the one who pardons, and the stating is the stating that allows either of the two, the atonement for the forgiver or the atonement for the offender and the separate reward for the forgiver.

**The Wrongdoer Who Does Not Judge**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the one who does not judge by what God revealed, and the state is the state of the wrongdoer in the name that the verse gives. The name is the second of the three names that the three verses of the account give to the state, the first being the name of the disbeliever in the verse of the Torah and the third being the name of the iniquitous in the verse of the Gospel, and the three names together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the state of the one who does not judge by the book. The verse of the retribution puts the state in the name of the wrongdoer, and the putting is the act that the verse does in the middle of the three, since the matter of the retribution is the matter that stands between the matter of the penalty of the fornication and the matter of the judgment of the Gospel, and the name is the name that the matter takes in the middle of the three.

**The Equivalence as the Measure of the Law**

The law of the retribution that the verse states is the law that the measure of the equivalence keeps, and the equivalence is the act that the law does in the form of the taking of the like for the like. The measure is the measure that removes the excess from the act of the retaliation, and the removing is the act that the law does in the form of the limit, since the retaliation that is the act of the vengeance is the act that the law does not give, and the retaliation that is the act of the measure is the act that the law gives. The law of the community of the sūrah carries the same principle in the verses that name the law of the retaliation, and the carrying is the fact that the commentators state in the relation of the two laws, since the principle of the equivalence is the principle that the two laws keep in the form that each of the two gives it. The forgoing that the verse states as the atonement is the act that the principle allows in the second form, and the two forms together are the law that the verse keeps for the community of the book and the community of the sūrah.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:46
> Then in the footsteps of the prophets, We sent Jesus, son of Mary, confirming the Torah revealed before him. And We gave him the Gospel containing guidance and light and confirming what was revealed in the Torah—a guide and a lesson to the God-fearing.

**Expanded Commentary**

**In the Footsteps of the Prophets**

The verse opens with the phrase that places the prophet in the line of the prophets, and the line is the line of the prophets who submitted that the verse of the Torah named. The footsteps are the footsteps of the line, and the placing is the act by which the sūrah states the succession of the prophets in the form of the line, since the prophet of this verse is the prophet who comes after the prophets of the community in the order that the line keeps. The verse does not state the years of the interval between the two, and the not stating is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the line, since the line is the line of the prophets and not the line of the years, and the interval is the matter of the account that the sūrah states in the verse of the address to the People of the Book.

**The Confirmation Stated Twice**

The verse states the confirmation in the two forms, and the two are the confirmation of the prophet and the confirmation of the book. Jesus is described as the one who confirms the Torah, and the Gospel is described as the book that confirms the Torah, and the two statements are the two forms of the same fact, which is the fact that the second of the two stands in the relation of the confirmation to the first. The Book describes the prophet in the same form in the verses where he is named as the one who confirms the book of Moses, and the description of the forerunner in the same form is the description that the Book gives in the verse where the one who comes before is named. The confirmation, in the reading the exegetes give, is the upholding of the validity of the rulings of the Torah that are not the rulings the Gospel abrogates specifically, and the upholding is the act that the confirmation does in the form of the keeping of the law, since the prophet who confirms the book is the prophet who keeps the law of the book in the measure that the book of the confirmation allows.

**The Book That Is the Light**

The Gospel is described in this verse in the form that the Torah was described in the verse before, and the form is the form of the guidance and the light. The two descriptions are the two forms of the same statement that the sūrah gives to the two books, and the statement is the statement that the scripture is the book that guides and the book that gives the light. The sūrah describes the book that is revealed to the prophet of the sūrah in the same form in the verse of the address where the light and the clear book are named, and the three descriptions together are the form the sūrah keeps for the three books in the account of the confirmation, which is the form of the book that confirms the book that is confirmed.

**A Guide and a Lesson for the God-Fearing**

The verse closes with the statement of the function of the Gospel in the form of the two names, and the two are the guide and the lesson. The guide is the act of the book that leads the one who follows in the way that the book gives, and the lesson is the act of the book that puts the warning and the reminder in the heart of the one who receives, and the two together are the function of the book in the form the book does it. The one for whom the two are the function is the one who is the God-fearing, and the naming of the one is the act that the verse does in the closing, since the guide and the lesson are the things the book gives to the one who fears, and the one who fears is the one who takes the two in the form the two are given.

**The Line of the Confirmation**

The verse of the Gospel stands in the sūrah in the middle of the line that the account keeps, and the line is the line of the confirmation that runs from the book of Moses to the book of the prophet and to the book of the sūrah. The verse of the Torah states the revelation of the first book and the judging by it, and this verse states the revelation of the second book and the confirmation of the first, and the verse of the Book states the revelation of the third and the confirmation of the two, and the three together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the line of the books. The confirmation is the act that the later book does to the earlier book, and the act is the act of the upholding of the validity and the guarding of the teaching, and the line is the line that the three books form in the order that the revelation gives. The sūrah states the line in the three verses, and the stating is the act by which the account of the judgment is told in the form of the line, since the judgment of the community is the judgment by the book of the line, and the book of the line is the book that confirms the book that came before it in the form that the sūrah states.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:47
> So let the people of the Gospel judge by what Allah has revealed in it. And those who do not judge by what Allah has revealed are ˹truly˺ the rebellious.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Command to Judge by the Book**

The verse states the command in the form that the verse of the Torah stated its command, and the form is the form of the judging by what God revealed in the book. The command is the command to the community of the Gospel, and the book that the command names is the book of the community, which is the Gospel in the relation that the Torah stands in to the community of the Torah. The sūrah keeps the two commands in the parallel form, and the parallel is the act by which the sūrah states the standing of the two books in the two communities, since each of the two is the book that the community of the book judges by, and the judging is the act that the standing of the book requires.

**The Torah Under the Gospel**

The exegetes note that the command to judge by what God revealed in the Gospel means, in most of the cases, the following of the rulings of the Torah, and the note is the note that the account of the prophet gives. The prophet of the Gospel lived largely by the rulings of the Torah, and the exception is the ruling that the Gospel abrogates, and the Gospel itself is the book that contains the teachings and the exhortations in the full measure and the legal rulings in the small measure, and the two together are the content of the book that the command names. The community that judges by the book in the form the exegetes state is the community that follows the law of the two books in the order that the two keep, and the order is the order of the confirmation that the verse before stated for the book and the prophet.

**The Validity That Stands and the Reading That Does Not**

The verse states the validity of the Gospel as the source of the guidance for the community of the Gospel, and the validity is the validity that the verse of the Torah states for the Torah in the verse before, and the two together are the sūrah’s position on the standing of the two scriptures. Some of the commentators argue that the command to follow the Gospel is the command that held before the coming of the book of the sūrah, and that the book of the sūrah abrogated the books that came before it, and the argument is the one the reading of the abrogation gives. The argument is not the argument that the context of the sūrah supports, since the verse of the question states why the community of the Torah came to the prophet for the judgment when the book of the Torah was the book they had, and the question is the question that presupposes the standing of the book in the presence of the prophet. The ruling of the prophet in the case of the two, which was the implementation of the penalty that the book of the Torah prescribed, was stated by the prophet as the act of the revival of the original teaching of the book, and the revival is the act that the standing of the book requires in the presence of the prophet.

**The Rebellious Who Do Not Judge**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the one who does not judge by what God revealed, and the state is the state of the rebellious in the name that the verse gives. The name is the third of the three names that the three verses of the account give to the state, and the three names together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the state of the one who does not judge by the book, which is the form of the disbeliever and the wrongdoer and the rebellious in the order that the three verses state. The verse of the Gospel puts the state in the last of the three names, and the putting is the act that the verse does at the end of the account of the two books, since the account of the judgment runs from the Torah to the Gospel and the name of the state runs from the first of the three to the last, and the two run together in the form the sūrah keeps.

**A Command That Stands Between the Two**

The position of the verse in the account is the position that gives it the function in the structure of the sūrah, and the structure is the structure of the three commands that the account keeps. The command of the verse of the Torah is the command to the community of the Torah to judge by the book of the Torah, and the command of this verse is the command to the community of the Gospel to judge by the book of the Gospel, and the command of the verse of the Book is the command to the prophet to judge between the two by what God revealed, and the three together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the account of the judgment. The sūrah puts the two commands of the two communities before the command of the prophet, and the putting is the act by which the account states the standing of the two books in the presence of the Book, since the standing is the standing of the books that the communities judge by, and the Book is the book that stands over the two in the form of the confirmation and the authority. The account of the judgment is the account that the sūrah tells in the three commands, and the three commands together are the structure that the sūrah gives to the matter of the judging by the book.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:48
> We have revealed to you ˹O Prophet˺ this Book with the truth, as a confirmation of previous Scriptures and a supreme authority on them. So judge between them by what Allah has revealed, and do not follow their desires over the truth that has come to you. To each of you We have ordained a code of law and a way of life. If Allah had willed, He would have made you one community, but His Will is to test you with what He has given ˹each of˺ you. So compete with one another in doing good. To Allah you will all return, then He will inform you ˹of the truth˺ regarding your differences.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Book That Confirms and Stands Over**

The verse states the revelation of the Book in the two forms, and the two are the confirmation of the book that came before and the standing over the book as the authority. The confirmation is the form the Book gives to itself in the several verses that name the act of the confirming of the earlier scriptures, and the standing over is the form that the word of the authority carries, which is the form of the one who testifies to the validity of the earlier scriptures and serves as the trustee and the keeper and the guardian of them. The word of the authority is also one of the Names of God, as the verse of the list of the Names states it among the King and the Most Holy and the Watcher (59:23), and the naming of the Book with the name of God is the act that the verse does in the form of the standing, since the Book that stands over the books is the Book that carries the name of the one who stands over the creation.

**The Command and the Two Readings of It**

The verse commands the prophet to judge between the two communities by what God revealed, and the command is the command that the two readings take in the two senses. In the reading of the majority of the commentators, the command is the command to judge according to what God revealed to the prophet, which is the Book of the sūrah, and the reading is the one that the form of the address supports. In the other reading, the command is the command to judge the two communities according to what God revealed to them, which is the book of the two, and the reading is the one the incident of the account shows, since the prophet judged the case of the two by the ruling of the book of the Torah in the act that he stated as the revival of the teaching of the book. The command not to follow the desires of the two is the command that the two readings take in the two forms, which is the form of the not ruling in accordance with the digressions and the alterations of the two in their own law, and the form of the not complying with the desire of the two to alter or neglect what came to the prophet in the Book. The sūrah repeats the warning in the verse that follows and in the verses of the other sūrahs that name the same warning against the following of the desires.

**A Law and a Way for Each Community**

The verse states the fact that God appointed for each of the communities a law and a way, and the fact is the fact that the verse makes the statement of, and the statement is the statement that goes beyond the two communities to the statement of the all. The word of the law is the word from the root that is the root of the technical term of the religious law, and the word of the way is the word that denotes the path that is smooth and clear, and the two together are the form of the appointment that the verse states for the community. The readings of the verse divide over the sense of the appointment, and the division is the one the word allows. In the reading of some of the commentators, the laws and the ways are not the laws and the ways that stand at the same time, and God appoints the law and the way for each of the eras. In the reading of the minority, the law and the way are the one law and the one way of the religion of the sūrah. In the reading of the commentator of the later period, the word of the Book is left out in the phrase but is meant, and the phrase is read as the appointment of the Book as the law and the way for each of the communities. The readings that hold the one law are not the readings that the implication of the verse supports, since the implication is the implication that the plurality of the communities is the will of God, and the will is the will the next line of the verse states.

**The Plurality as the Will and the Test**

The verse states the fact of the plurality in the form of the counterfactual, and the counterfactual is the statement that God did not will the one community, and the one community is the community that does not exist in the fact of the creation. The fact of the plurality of the communities is the fact the Book states in the several verses that name the single community that was and the difference that came, including the verse that states that humanity was one community and then differed (2:213), and the verse that states that had the Lord willed, he would have made them one community and they would have differed (10:19, 11:118, 16:93, 42:8). The verse states the purpose of the plurality in the form of the test, and the test is the test of the obedience in what God gave, and the giving is the giving of the law and the way that the verse before stated. The plurality of the religious forms is the matter of the divine wisdom in the same form that the other modes of the human difference are the matters of the wisdom, and the modes are the gender and the race and the standing of the society, as the verse of the peoples and the tribes states in the form of the knowing of one another. The sūrah of the change of the direction of the prayer is the example the commentators give of the religious difference as the test, since the direction that was appointed for the community of the sūrah was the direction different from the direction of the community of the book, and the difference was the difference by which God knew the one who follows from the one who turns back. The commentators who read the forms as the forms of the eras take the test in the sense of the distinguishing of the faithful from the one who disbelieves in the form that was appointed for the time, and the reading is the one the interpretation of the supersession gives.

**The Competition That the Verse Commands**

The command that closes the verse is the command to compete in the doing of the good, and the command is the command the verse states for the human being as the whole, and the statement is the statement that the reading of the majority gives. The competition is the competition that the contemporaneous existence of the different communities makes possible, and the possibility is the possibility the reading of the supersession does not give, since the competition is the competition in the doing and not the competition in the claiming. The competition is in the good deeds, and the deeds are the acts of the practice and not the acts of the theology, and the verse states the competition in the form that the practice takes, since the resolving of the differences of the theology is not the work of the one who holds the religion in this world, and the differences are the differences that God resolves in the next, where the informing of the matter they differ about is the act that the verse states at the close. The verse stands in the Book as the verse that carries the weight of the question of the plurality of the religions, and the weight is the weight that the verse gives to the statement of the validity of the different forms and the appointment of the differences by the will of God, and the two together are the ground that the community of the sūrah stands on in the matter of the community that is not the community of the sūrah.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:49
> And judge between them ˹O Prophet˺ by what Allah has revealed, and do not follow their desires. And beware, so they do not lure you away from some of what Allah has revealed to you. If they turn away ˹from Allah’s judgment˺, then know that it is Allah’s Will to repay them for some of their sins, and that many people are indeed rebellious.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Command Repeated in the Account**

The verse repeats the command that the verse before stated, and the repetition is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the insistence. The command is the command to the prophet to judge between the two communities by what God revealed, and the verse states it again after the statement of the law and the way that was appointed for each of the communities. Many of the major commentators note that the command is the command to judge by the scripture of the community of the sūrah alone, and the note is the one that the form of the address gives, since the address is the address to the prophet and the book that was revealed to the prophet is the book of the community of the sūrah. The repetition of the command is the act that the account does so that the command stands in the account in the form of the command that is given twice, and the twice is the act that the sūrah does at the point where the account of the judgment turns to the account of the warning.

**The Warning That the Verse Adds**

The verse adds the warning that the verse before did not state, and the warning is the warning against the turning away of the prophet from part of what God revealed to him. The account that the early commentators preserve connects the warning to the incident that is the reported incident of the group of the authorities of the community who tried to persuade the prophet to side with them in the dispute that they had with some of their fellow coreligionists in the city, and the group included the one who was the authority of the case of the two fornicators in the account of the beginning of the sūrah. The tempting was the tempting of the promise, which is the promise that the authorities would become believers in the message and confirm the truth of the prophethood, and the wider population would follow them in the matter, and the tempting is the act that the sūrah warns the prophet against in the form of the warning, since the tempting is the act that seeks to take the judgment of the prophet in the direction that the tempting wants and not in the direction that the book of the prophet requires.

**The Prophet and the Possibility of the Lapse**

Some of the commentators take the warning as the statement of the fact that the prophet, although the prophet does not sin in the act of the intention, may have the lapse of the judgment or the error of the ruling, and the taking is the one that the word of the warning allows. The warning is, in the reading that the context of the sūrah supports, the warning that relates to the concern of the verses of the account that the community of the book follow the rulings of the book as they were revealed, without the altering that the desire of the community produces, and the relating is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the warning to the prophet that is the warning to the account. Another of the readings takes the warning in the sense that the community of the book might mislead the prophet into the ruling that is not the ruling by the lying about the true content of the book of Moses and the penalties that the book prescribes, and the lying is the act that the reading names as the form of the misleading, since the misleading by the lie is the form of the tempting that is the hardest of the forms to guard against, and the guarding is the act that the verse of the warning commands the prophet to do.

**The Consolation in the Turning**

The verse states the consolation in the form of the knowledge, and the knowledge is the knowledge that if the community turns away from the ruling of the prophet, the turning is the act that God intends to strike them for in the matter of some of their sins. The striking is the act that the verse states as the act of God, and the statement is the act by which the sūrah consoles the prophet in the form of the fact, since the fact that the turning of the community is the act that the sin of the community produces is the fact that the prophet is to know, and the knowing is the act that the sūrah gives him in the form of the command of the knowing. One of the commentators takes the statement of the striking as the proof of the principle that all good and evil comes about through the will of God for some ultimate purpose, and the taking is the one that the statement of the intending of God gives, since the intending is the act that the verse states for the striking in the form of the purpose.

**The Structure of the Account at the Turning**

The sūrah keeps the command, the warning, and the consolation in the verse of the account, and the three together are the structure that the account keeps at the point where the account of the judgment turns to the account of the state of the community. The command is the command of the judging, the warning is the warning of the turning of the prophet, and the consolation is the consolation of the turning of the community, and the three are the three acts that the sūrah states for the prophet in the form of the address. The account of the judgment is the account that the sūrah has told from the verse of the case of the two fornicators to the verse of the Book that confirms, and the verse of this account is the verse where the account turns from the telling of the judgment to the telling of the state of the one who judges and the state of the community that is judged, and the two states together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the account of the judging.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:50
> Is it the judgment of ˹pre-Islamic˺ ignorance they seek? Who could be a better judge than Allah for people of sure faith?

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Judgment of the Age Named**

The verse names the age in the form of the judgment, and the judgment is the judgment of the age that is the age of the ignorance. The age is the era in the land that was before the coming of the religion of the sūrah, and the era is the era marked by the idolatry and the culpable ignorance of the true religion, and the ignorance is the ignorance that the Book uses in the verses that name the age in the form of the word. The judgment of the age is the judgment that is not guided by any religious law, and the not being guided is the state that the judgment of the age takes, since the judgment that is not guided by the law is the judgment that is guided by the opinion of the human being and the whim of the one who judges, and the two together are the form that the judgment of the age takes in the absence of the law.

**The Standing That the Judgment Takes**

The reference of the verse to the judgment of the age may be the reference that is particular to the matter of the differential penalties for the killing, and the matter is the matter that was meted out among the clans of the community in accordance with the social standing of the one who kills and the one who is killed. The judgment that takes the standing into the account is the judgment that was the characteristic of the tribal system in the land, and the characteristic is the act that the system did in the form of the adjustment of the penalty, since the penalty of the system is the penalty that the standing of the two gives to the act. The judgment is not the judgment that the law of God allows, and the not allowing is the act that the verse of the justice states, since the verse of the justice commands the standing firm for the justice in the witness for God even if the witness is against the one himself or the parents or the close relatives, and whether the two are the rich or the poor, and the command is the command that the judgment of the age does not take, and the not taking is the act that the verse of this sūrah rebukes in the form of the question.

**The Rebuke of the Seeking**

The verse is the rebuke of the community that seeks the judgment that is the judgment of the age, and the seeking is the act of the one who rejects the judgment of the prophet because the judgment did not accord with the desire of the community. The rebuke is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the question, and the question is the form of the rebuke that the sūrah keeps for the act of the seeking, since the question that names the judgment of the age is the question that puts the seeking of the community in the form of the seeking of the judgment that is the hallmark of the age of the idolatry. The hallmark is the act that the judgment of the age does in the form of the ignorance of the law of God and the justice and the benefit and the mercy that the law of God carries, and the ignorance is the state that the verse states for the community in the form of the charge, since the community that seeks the judgment of the age is the community that is in the state of the ignorance of the law in the form that the age took it.

**The Question That Names the Fairer**

The second question of the verse is the question that names the one who is the fairer in the judgment, and the one is God in the form that the judgment of God takes. The question is the question that the sūrah states in the form of the answer that it gives, and the answer is the answer that the question does not allow the other than the one it names, since the one who is the fairer in the judgment than God is the one that the question does not give. The closing phrase of the verse puts the question in the form of the address to the people who are the certain, and the certain is the one who is in the state of the certainty of the belief, and the putting is the act that the verse does in the closing, since the question is the question that the certain takes as the question of the state of the community that seeks the judgment of the age, and the taking is the act that the sūrah expects of the one who is the certain.

**The Two Judgments in the Contrast**

The sūrah puts the two judgments in the verse in the form of the contrast, and the contrast is the contrast of the judgment of the age and the judgment of God. The judgment of the age is the judgment that the standing of the two gives, and the judgment of God is the judgment that the equivalence and the justice give, and the two are the two forms of the judgment that the sūrah keeps in the account of the case of the two fornicators. The account of the case is the account that the sūrah has told from the verse of the coming to the prophet for the judgment to the verse of this question, and the telling is the telling that puts the choice of the judgment in the form of the question at the end, since the choice is the choice between the two judgments, and the two judgments are the judgments that the verse of the question names in the form of the one that is sought and the one that is the fairer.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:51
> O believers! Take neither Jews nor Christians as guardians—they are guardians of each other. Whoever does so will be counted as one of them. Surely Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Prohibition of the Guardianship**

The verse addresses the believers in the form of the prohibition, and the prohibition is the prohibition of the taking of the two communities as the guardians. The word that the verse uses is the word that can mean the friend and the ally, and the meaning that the context of the verse gives is the meaning of the one to whom the one turns as the protector or the dominant authority, since the context is the context of the community that is in the state of the struggle and the protection. The word and the noun from the same root are the words that the Book uses for the bonds of the loyalty and the mutual protection and the friendship that are the bonds that mark the relation between the members of the same religious community, and the bonds are the bonds that the verse of the guardians of one another states for the one who believes and emigrates and strives, and the bonds are the bonds that the verse of the believers as the men and the women states for the community of the belief. The prohibition of the verse is the prohibition of the taking of the bonds in the form that the bonds are not the bonds of the community of the belief, and the taking is the act that the verse forbids in the address to the believers.

**The Other Addresses of the Same Word**

This is the only verse of the Book that urges the believers not to take the two communities specifically as the guardians, and the specificity is the act that the verse does in the form of the naming. The believers are urged in the other verses to avoid taking as the guardians the ones who disbelieve, and the ones who mock the religion of the believers, as the verse of the sūrah that names the mocking states, and the enemies of God, as the verse of the address to the believers of the sūrah of the criterion states, and the close relatives who prefer the disbelief to the belief, as the verse of the prohibition of the taking of the parents and the siblings states. The verse of this sūrah puts the two communities in the form of the guardians of one another, and the putting is the act by which the sūrah states that the two realize the bonds of the loyalty among themselves as the separate religious communities, and the verse of the later account of the sūrah rebukes the two for having themselves taken the disbelievers as the guardians in the act that the verse names as the wicked deed.

**The Occasion the Accounts Give**

The accounts that the exegetes preserve state the occasion of the verse in the form of the alliance of the Muslims of the city with the clans of the community. One of the accounts tells of the companion who was one of the natives of the city who became a believer and kept the alliances of the mutual protection with the clans, and the companion came to the prophet to renounce the attachment to them and to declare that the only guardians of him were God, the prophet, and the believers, and the declaration is the declaration that the verse of the guardianship states in the form of the command. Another of the accounts tells of the one who came to the prophet declaring the need to maintain the relation of the protection with the allies of the community, and the verse came down instructing him and the believers in general not to do so. Another of the accounts reports that after the Muslims suffered the military loss at the place of the valley, some of the Muslims, feeling the vulnerability, established the allies among the clans of the community, and the verse was meant to forbid the practice, and the forbidding is the act that the verse does in the form of the prohibition.

**The State of the One Who Takes Them**

The verse states the state of the one who takes the two communities as the guardians, and the state is the state of the one who is of them, meaning one of them. The taking of the alliance of the mutual protection is the act that identifies the one who takes as the one of the community that is taken, since the relation of the mutual protection with the one who is of the same religion is the relation that defines the one, in part, as the member of the religious community. The verses that name the criticism of the taking of the allies apart from the believers are the verses that the sūrah and the other sūrahs state in the same form, and the criticism is the criticism that the verse of this sūrah states in the form of the naming of the state. Some of the commentators take the state in the sense that the status of the one who allies with the members of another religious community has the same status, in the law and in the theology, as the other members of the community, and the taking is the one that the word of the guardianship allows in the form of the belonging.

**The Measure the Verse Does Not Take**

The prohibition of the verse had much to do with the fluid and the precarious situation of the society of the community in the time of the prophet, and the situation is the situation of the community that struggles to define and establish itself against the enemies of the religion. The law of the religion, in the state where the state of the community became fully established, allowed the agreements of the mutual protection with the states and the entities that are not of the community, and the allowing is the act that the law does in the form of the treaty. The word of the verse is the word that is close to the protector and the ally and not the friend, and the reading that takes the word in the sense of the friend and doubts the acceptability of the amiable relations is the reading that the majority of the commentators and the account of the occasion do not give. The verse is not the verse that forbids the friendly relations on the level of the person, since the reading contradicts the verse of the marriage that allows the most intimate of the personal relations to exist between the believers and the believing women of the two communities, and the verse of the kindness that states that God does not forbid the dealing kindly and fairly with the ones who have not fought and have not driven out, and the two verses together are the measure that the sūrah keeps for the relations that are not the relations of the guardianship.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:52
> You see those with sickness in their hearts racing for their guardianship, saying ˹in justification˺, “We fear a turn of fortune will strike us.” But perhaps Allah will bring about ˹your˺ victory or another favour by His command, and they will regret what they have hidden in their hearts.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Disease in the Heart**

The verse describes the ones in the form of the disease that is in the heart, and the disease is the state that the Book uses for the impure intention and the deep religious hypocrisy and the doubt that the one harbors in the soul. The description is the description that the Book gives in the verse that names the impure intention, and the description is the description that the Book gives in the verses that name the hypocrite in the form of the one who is in the state of the doubt and the hiding. The verse may be the verse that addresses the specific case of the one who was named in the account of the previous verse, and the case is the case of the one who came to the prophet declaring the need to maintain the relation of the protection with the allies of the community. The more likely of the readings takes the verse as the verse that addresses the hypocrites in general, and the general is the form that the verse keeps for the state of the one who is in the disease of the heart.

**The Hedging of the Fear**

The verse states the act of the ones who are in the disease, and the act is the act of the hastening to the two communities in the form of the seeking of the alliance. The ones are the ones who fear the change of the fortune, and the fear is the fear that the verse states in the form of their own statement, which is the statement of the fearing that the change of the fortune may befall them. The seeking of the alliance is the act that the fear produces in the form of the hedging of the bet, and the hedging is the act of the one who establishes the supportive alliance with the ones outside the community who would protect them if the community suffered the adversity. The hastening is the act that the verse sees, and the seeing is the act that the address to the prophet does in the form of the pointing, since the pointing is the act by which the sūrah states the act of the ones in the state that the ones do not want to show, and the showing is the act that the hastening does in the form that the verse names.

**The Victory and the Command**

The verse states the two acts of God that may come to the ones, and the two are the victory and the command. The victory is the victory that the majority of the readings take as the future victory of the community of the belief over the religious opponents, and the taking is the one that the later reading connects to the conquest of the city in the year of the eighth of the migration, and the connecting is the one that the account of the conquest gives. The word of the victory is the word that can also mean the judgment, and the other reading is the reading that takes the word in the sense of the granting of the judgment, and many of the commentators reconcile the two by considering the conquest to be the manifesting of the ultimate judgment of God in favor of the believers over the ones of the idolatry. The command is the command that the early commentators take in the several senses, which is the sense of the implementation of the tax on the non-Muslim residents of the state, and the sense of the later expulsion of the two communities from the central regions of the land, and the sense of the sudden revealing of the insincere intentions of the hypocrites. One of the commentators takes the statement of the may of the verse as the statement of the promised certainty and not the mere possibility, and the taking is the one that the wording of the verse gives in the form that the wording takes.

**The Remorse That the Verse Promises**

The verse closes with the statement of the remorse, and the remorse is the remorse that the ones will have for the thing that they had been harboring in the souls. The harboring is the act that the ones do in the state of the disease, and the harboring is the act that the heart does when the heart is in the state that the disease of the verse names. The remorse is the act that the sūrah promises in the form of the certainty, and the certainty is the form that the verse keeps for the future of the ones, since the future of the one who is in the disease is the future of the one who regrets the act that the disease did in the form of the alliance. The sūrah states the remorse in the closing of the verse, and the stating is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the promise, and the promise is the promise that the account of the disease keeps for the community that hears the account, since the account of the disease is the account that the community is meant to take as the account of the state that the community can be in when the fear of the change of the fortune is the act that the heart does.

**The Verse in the Account of the Alliances**

The verse stands in the sūrah between the prohibition of the guardianship and the question of the believers, and the position is the position that gives the verse the function in the account of the alliances. The verse before is the verse that forbids the taking of the two communities as the guardians, and the verse after is the verse that states the question of the believers at the oath of the ones who swore, and this verse is the verse that tells the act of the ones who are in the disease in the form of the hastening to the two communities, and the three together are the account that the sūrah keeps for the matter of the improper alliance. The account is the account that the sūrah tells in the form of the prohibition and the telling and the question, and the three forms together are the structure that the sūrah gives to the account of the one who seeks the protection in the community that is not the community of the belief.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:53
> ˹Only then,˺ the believers will ask ˹one another˺, “Are these the ones who swore solemn oaths by Allah that they were with you?” Their deeds have been in vain, so they have become losers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Question of the Believers**

The verse states the question of the believers in the form of the surprise, and the surprise is the surprise at the hypocrisy of the ones who swore the most solemn oaths that they were with the believers. The oaths are the oaths of the solemnity that the verse names in the form of the most solemn of the oaths by God, and the swearing is the act that the ones did in the state that the oath did not show. The question is the expression of the surprise and the expression of the gratitude for the granting of the success of God to the ones who were the sincere in the belief, and the two expressions together are the form that the question keeps in the account. The command of the verse before, if the command is the command of the sudden revealing of the hidden insincerity, is the command that the question of the verse takes as the occasion of the surprise, and the revealing is the act that the question answers in the form of the pointing at the ones who are the revealed.

**The Other Address of the Question**

The other reading takes the question as the question that the believers address to the community of the book with whom some of the Muslims had sought the alliance, and the reading is the one that the verse of the later sūrah supports, since the verse of the later sūrah reports the hypocrites as the ones who vowed the protection for the allies of the community in the form of the statement that if the allies are expelled, the hypocrites will leave with them and will not obey anyone against them and will help them if they are fought against. The question in this reading is the question of the pointing at the ones who are the ones the believers sought the alliance with, and the pointing is the act that the question does in the form of the surprise, since the surprise is the surprise at the state of the ones who swore that they were with the believers and were not the ones who were with the believers. The two readings of the question are the two forms that the word allows, and the two forms together are the account that the sūrah keeps for the oath of the ones in the matter of the alliance.

**The Deeds That Come to Naught**

The verse states the fact of the deeds that come to naught in the form of the statement, and the statement is the statement that the teaching of the Book keeps in the form of the requirement of the belief and the good deeds together for the salvation. The Book requires the two together in the verses that name the state of the one who believes and does the good, and the some of the schools of the theology accept the possibility of the eventual salvation through the belief alone, and the most of the schools consider the deeds as the indication of the belief or the relation of the belief, and all agree on the basis of this verse and the similar verses that the deeds in the absence of the proper belief are the deeds that are meaningless on the day of the judgment and come to naught. The verses that name the coming to naught of the deeds are the verses that the Book states in the several sūrahs, and the stating is the act that the Book does in the form of the repetition of the statement, since the statement of the naught is the statement that the Book keeps for the deed that is not the deed of the belief. The deeds that come to naught are the deeds that merit no reward and have no value in the salvation, and the two together are the state that the verse states for the deeds of the one who is not the one of the belief.

**The Loser in the Deeper Sense**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the one who does the good deeds without the belief, and the state is the state of the loser in the next world. The loser is the one who perishes for the lack of the belief despite the deeds, and the perishing is the act that the verse states in the form of the naming of the state. In the deeper sense that the teaching of the sūrah gives, the thing that the one considers the good deed is not the thing that is the good deed unless the deed is the deed that is joined with the belief, and the joining is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the requirement. The requirement is the requirement that the sūrah keeps for the deed in the form of the deed that the belief gives it, and the giving is the act that the belief does to the deed in the form that the deed becomes the deed that the next world takes as the deed of the one who is the one of the belief.

**The Oath That Becomes the Charge**

The sūrah puts the oath of the ones in the form of the charge in the question of the believers, and the charge is the charge of the state that the oath did not show. The oath is the oath that the ones gave in the state of the sincerity that the oath did not carry, and the not carrying is the act that the sūrah states in the form of the question, since the question that names the oath is the question that puts the oath in the form of the thing that the coming to naught of the deeds negates. The oath and the deed together are the form of the claim that the ones made, and the claim is the claim of the being with the believers, and the coming to naught of the deeds is the act that the sūrah does to the claim in the form of the negation, since the claim of the one who is not the one of the belief is the claim that the deeds of the one do not carry in the next world.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:54
> O believers! Whoever among you abandons their faith, Allah will replace them with others who love Him and are loved by Him. They will be humble with the believers but firm towards the disbelievers, struggling in the Way of Allah; fearing no blame from anyone. This is the favour of Allah. He grants it to whoever He wills. And Allah is All-Bountiful, AllKnowing.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Exchange That the Verse Keeps**

The verse opens with the statement that the abandoning of the faith by any of the believers is the act that does not injure God or the religion in the end, and the statement is given in the form of the exchange, which is the act by which God puts in their place the people who are bound to Him by the bond of the mutual love. The place of the verse in the sūrah, where it stands at the close of the account of the alliances with the members of the two communities, may hold the meaning that such an alliance is, in some of the cases, the act that is the same as the leaving of the faith, and the holding is the one that the root of the word carries, since the word of the abandoning is the word from the root that is the root of the word of the leaving. The meaning is not one that the verse carries in the absolute form, since the prophet and the first authorities of the community kept the treaty relations with the powers and the groups outside the community, and the ruler of the people who sheltered the early group of the believers before the migration was the ruler of the other faith, and the sheltering is the act that the verse does not put in the form of the forbidding. The matter that the verse points at is the matter that rises when the alliance is the alliance that carries the danger of the splitting of the believers or the betraying of the community by its members or in favor of the enemies, and the two dangers together are the state that the verse keeps for the alliance that is the state of the abandoning.

**The Tribes the Verse Foresees**

The major commentators read the verse as the foretelling of the leaving of the faith by some of the tribes of the Arabs in the later years of the life of the prophet, and the foretelling is the act that the verse does in the form of the promise of the exchange. The leaving is also the leaving of the allegiance by the tribes who turned from the caliphate of the city once the prophet had passed from this world, and the turning is the act that the foretelling of the verse names in the form of the fact that the history keeps. The verse states the leaving in the form of the promise of the exchange, and the promise is the one that the sūrah keeps for the community in the form of the assurance, since the assurance that the community is the community which God fills with the sincere ones when the sincere ones turn away is the assurance that the address of the verse gives to the believer.

**The Ones Who Take Their Place**

The commentators divide over the identity of the people whom God brings in their place, and the division is the one that the several readings of the verse keep. Many of the readings take the people as the first of the caliphs, who met the tribes that had left with the fighting and brought them back, and many note that the people may be the companion of the Ashʿarī line and the tribes of the southern land in general, who carried the major part of the conquests under the second of the caliphs, on the ground of the report of the tradition. The interpretations that are less widely reported take the people as the Helpers of the city, or as the people of the Persian who was the companion, also on the ground of the report, and the report is the one that stands behind the reading less widely than the one of the companion. Some of the readings raise the possibility that the reference is to the one who was the cousin and the son-in-law of the prophet and the fourth of the caliphs, and the reading rests on the report of the standard at the battle of the well, since the prophet gave the standard to the one on the morning of the battle and said that the standard would go to the one whose love is with God and His messenger and whose love is the love of God and His messenger. In the end, the reference is the general one as well as the historical one, and it is meant to point at the fact that God does not run short of the sincere servants, whatever the perfidy and the hypocrisy of the others may be. The interpretations of the verse were at times taken into the dispute of the two parties of the community over the merit of the first of the caliphs and the one who was the cousin, and the dispute led some of the commentators to set down the long defense of the attribution of the verse to either of the two, on the ground of the one who best fits the other marks that the verse names. One of the later commentators argues that the verse is among the strongest of the proofs against the extreme claim of the party that the companions who confirmed the caliphate of the first and passed by the authority of the other had in fact left the faith, and the argument rests on the promise of the exchange, since the promise names the leaving as the act that is met by the exchange, and the companions were not the ones who were exchanged, and the not being exchanged puts the claim in the form of the thing that does not stand.

**The Love That Runs Both Ways**

The phrase of the verse that names the people whom God loves and who love Him is the phrase that the commentators discuss in the form of the love that runs both ways between God and the sincere servants. The Book sets down the several statements about the ones whom God loves, and they are the virtuous, the patient, and the just, and about the ones whose love He does not carry, and they are the sinners and the transgressors, and the love of God as the mark of the believers is the mark that the verse of the love states in the form of the statement that the true believers love God above all.
