**The Two Acts of the Community**

The verse names the two acts of the community in the form of the making open and the concealing, and the two acts are the acts that the community does in the state of the relation of the community to the message of the messenger. The making open is the act of the one who puts the state of the belief or the state of the disbelief in the form of the showing to the community, and the concealing is the act of the one who keeps the state of the belief or the state of the disbelief in the form of the hiding from the community, and the two together are the form that the verse keeps for the state of the community in the matter of the message, since the state of the community is the state that the two acts do in the form of the showing and the hiding of the state of the heart of the one who is in the relation to the message. The knowledge of God for the two acts is the knowledge that the sūrah states in the form of the statement of the state of God in the matter of the community, and the stating is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the awareness of the knowledge of God for the open and the hidden.

**The Verse in the Account of the Proclamation**

The sūrah keeps this verse in the place where it follows the verse of the two sets of the qualities of God at the close of the account of the pilgrimage, and the following is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the placing of the verse of the duty of the messenger after the close of the account of the prohibitions. The sūrah states the duty of the messenger in the two places of the sūrah, and the two places are the place of the command of the obeying in the account of the prohibitions and the place of this verse at the close of the account of the pilgrimage, and the stating of the duty in the two places is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the repetition of the statement of the state of the messenger in the matter of the message, since the repetition of the statement is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the awareness of the state of the messenger at the two turning points of the sūrah, which are the turning point of the account of the prohibitions and the turning point of the account of the pilgrimage.

**The Address of the State of the Heart**

The sūrah puts the verse in the form of the address of the state of the heart of the community, and the addressing is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the statement of the knowledge of God for the open and the hidden of the community. The state of the heart of the community is the state that the verse puts in the form of the thing that the knowledge of God reaches in the act of the making open and the act of the concealing, and the reaching of the knowledge of God is the act that the verse does in the form of the statement of the state of God in the matter of the heart of the community, since the statement of the knowledge of God for the open and the hidden is the statement that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the awareness of the state of the heart before God, and the awareness of the state of the heart is the act that the verse does in the form of the address of the community at the close of the account of the pilgrimage in the middle of the sūrah.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:100
> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “Good and evil are not equal, though you may be dazzled by the abundance of evil. So be mindful of Allah, O people of reason, so you may be successful.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Inequality That the Book Keeps**

The verse states the inequality of the good and the evil in the form of the saying of the messenger to the community, and the inequality is the state that the verse names for the two in the form of the not being of the equality. This is one of the many places of the Book where the Book asserts the spiritual and the qualitative inequality of the human beings in the several ways, and the asserting is the act that the Book does in the form of the repetition of the statement of the inequality in the verses of the other sūrahs. The verse of the sūrah of the story of the night states the question of the equality of the one who knows and the one who does not know, and the question is the act that the sūrah of the story does in the form of the putting of the two states of the knowledge and the not knowing in the form of the question of the inequality, and the question of the inequality is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the address of the possessors of the intellect to the reflecting on the state of the two, and the stating of the inequality in the several verses is the act that the Book does in the form of the keeping of the statement of the inequality of the human beings in the spiritual and the qualitative form.

**The Abundance of the Evil in the Readings**

The verse states the abundance of the evil in the form of the thing that may impress the community, and the impressing is the act that the abundance does in the form of the state of the sight of the community. Although the evil may exist in the great quantity, the good surpasses the evil in the spiritual quality, and the surpassing is the act that the verse states in the form of the statement of the inequality, since the quality of the good is the quality that the verse keeps in the form of the thing that is not the thing of the quantity. The report of the tradition states that the human beings are like the hundred of the camels, and that the one can hardly find among them the camel that is the fit for the riding, and the stating is the act that the report does in the form of the naming of the state of the human beings in the matter of the fit for the riding, and the naming of the state is the act that the report does in the form of the support of the statement of the verse regarding the abundance of the evil and the scarcity of the good. Some of the commentators take the comparison of the evil and the good in the verse in the reference to the things that are the evil and the good, which are the things that are the unlawful and the lawful, rather than in the reference to the people, and the taking is the act that the two commentators do in the form of the reading of the two words in the verse, since the reading of the things is the reading that the word allows in the form of the reference to the things that are the unlawful and the lawful in the matter of the abundance and the scarcity.

**The Possessors of the Intellect**

The phrase of the possessors of the intellect is the phrase that the Book uses in the several verses of the other sūrahs, and the using is the act that the Book does in the form of the repetition of the phrase in the verses that name the state of the one who is in the intellect. The Book addresses the possessors of the intellect directly in the several places, and the addressing is the act that the Book does in the form of the putting of the possessors of the intellect in the form of the object of the address in the verse of the sūrah of the family that states the security of the life in the law of the retaliation for the possessors of the intellect, and the verse of the sūrah of the dispensation that commands the fearing of God for the possessors of the intellect and the faith. The word of the intellect is the word that has the primary meaning of the inner essence of the thing, and the secondary meaning of the pure intellect or the heart in the form that these represent the purest spiritual essence of the human soul, and the two meanings together are the form that the commentary keeps for the word. The possessors of the intellect are said, in the more literal form, to possess the knowledge of the inner essence of the things, and the knowledge is the knowledge that must be discerned with the intellect rather than with the reason or the senses, and the discerning with the intellect is the act that the possessors of the intellect do in the form of the state of the faculty of the attainment of the knowledge that is directly connected to God, through which the one acquires the knowledge of the essential realities, whereas the reason is the faculty of the discernment that arrives at the knowledge through the logical and the analytical and the dialectical process that is the dualistic in the approach. One of the commentators of the later period glosses the possessors of the intellect in this verse as the possessors of the pure hearts, and describes them as the ones who keep themselves from the evil even when the evil is the many, and who turn themselves to the good even when the good is the few, and the glossing is the act that the commentator does in the form of the naming of the state of the possessors of the intellect in the matter of the evil and the good.

**The Command of the Fear of God**

The verse states the command of the fearing of God in the form of the address of the possessors of the intellect, and the command is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the possessors of the intellect in the state of the fearing of God. The purpose of the command is the purpose of the prospering of the possessors of the intellect, and the prospering is the act that the sūrah states in the form of the naming of the state of the one who is in the fearing of God in the matter of the outcome of the life, since the state of the prospering is the state that the verse keeps for the one who takes the command of the fearing of God in the form of the act, and the command of the fearing of God is the command that the sūrah does in the form of the address of the possessors of the intellect at the close of the statement of the inequality of the good and the evil.

**The Verse in the Account of the Sūrah**

The sūrah keeps this verse in the place where it follows the verse of the duty of the messenger and the knowledge of God for the open and the hidden, and the following is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the placing of the verse of the inequality at the point where the sūrah turns from the account of the duty of the messenger to the account of the state of the community in the matter of the good and the evil. The verse of the inequality is the verse that the sūrah does in the form of the saying of the messenger to the community in the matter of the good and the evil, and the saying is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the taking of the inequality of the two in the form of the act of the fearing of God, since the taking of the inequality is the act that the command of the verse does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the preferring of the good over the evil in the matter of the quality and not in the matter of the quantity, and the preferring of the good is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the close of the account of the state of the community in the middle of the sūrah.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:101
> O believers! Do not ask about any matter which, if made clear to you, may disturb you. But if you inquire about what is being revealed in the Quran, it will be made clear to you. Allah has forgiven what was done ˹in the past˺. And Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Forbearing.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Account of the Question of the Pilgrimage**

The account that is the most widely attested about the revealing of the verse reports that the verse came in the response to the man who, when the messenger announced that the pilgrimage was the incumbent upon the believers, asked whether the pilgrimage was the required of the community every year, and the asking is the act that the man did in the form of the question of the measure of the pilgrimage. The messenger passed by the question three times before the answering of it, and the passing by is the act that the messenger did in the form of the not answering of the question in the three times, and the answer of the messenger was the answer that the pilgrimage was not the required every year, and the statement that if the messenger had said the yes, the pilgrimage would have become the obligatory for the community in the form of the every year, and the statement is the act that the four commentators keep in the form of the report of the answer. In the other version of the answer, the messenger added the saying that the one is to keep silent about the things about which the messenger keeps silent, and that the people who came before the community were the ones that the questions and the disputing with the prophets destroyed, and the saying is the act that the commentator keeps in the form of the report of the addition, and the addition is the act that the report does in the form of the echoing of the warning of the verse after, since the warning of the verse after is the warning of the people before who asked and became the disbelievers.

**The Mercy in the Ambiguity of the Command**

The answer of the messenger suggests that there may be the intentional leniency or the mercy in the ambiguities of the certain religious commands, and the suggesting is the act that the answer does in the form of the state of the ambiguity. If the one pushes to have the ambiguity clarified, the clarification may make the obligation the more difficult or the too difficult for the community, and the making difficult is the act that the clarification does in the form of the state of the obligation in the matter of the community, since the state of the obligation is the state that the clarification of the ambiguity does in the form of the thing that the community is to do in the measure that the clarification gives. The people are to refrain from the asking of the questions of this nature, and the refraining is the act that the commentator states in the form of the meaning of the verse, since the refraining of the asking is the act that the community is to do in the form of the keeping of the ambiguity in the state of the mercy, and the keeping of the ambiguity is the act that the community does in the form of the not pushing of the clarification that would trouble the community if the matter were made clear to it.

**The Other Occasions the Reports Keep**

The other of the reports indicates that the verses took the revealing in the response to the ones who asked the messenger the questions that are not the sincere in order to test or belittle the messenger, and the asking of the questions is the act that the ones did in the form of the testing and the belittling of the messenger. The ones asked the messenger about the things that would naturally be the unknown to the messenger, such as the question of the father of the one or the question of the place where the lost camel of the one wandered, and the asking of the two questions is the act that the three commentators name as the form of the state of the ones, since the answers of the two questions, especially the first of the two, may reveal the troubling information that is the better left in the state of the unknown. The other of the reports indicates that the verses took the revealing after some of the people asked the messenger about the practices of the idolatry that the sūrah of this account names and refutes in the verse after, and the asking about the practices is the act that the two commentators name as the form of the state of the ones. The general warning of the verse regarding the asking of the questions whose answers may trouble the community is the warning that applies to all of the three types of the questions, and the applying is the act that the reading does in the form of the putting of the warning in the form of the general, and the warning is read in the connection with the statement of the verse of the command of the obeying that only the clear proclamation is the incumbent upon the messenger, and the reading in the connection is the act that the commentator does in the form of the joining of the two verses, since the joining of the two verses is the act that the account does in the form of the statement of the state of the messenger in the matter of the knowledge, which is the state of the one whose duty is the proclamation and not the state of the one who has the knowledge of the things that are not the revealed to him. The Book states repeatedly that neither the messenger of the sūrah nor the earlier prophets had the access to the knowledge of the unseen beyond what was revealed to them, and the stating is the act that the Book does in the form of the repetition of the statement of the state of the knowledge of the prophets in the verses of the other sūrahs.

**The Excessive Questioning and the Legitimate Question**

In the more general way, some of the commentators indicate that the verses are meant to discourage the excessive questioning of any type, and the indicating is the act that the commentator does in the form of the reading of the verses in the general sense. The report of the tradition states that one of the three things that God dislikes among the people is the too much of the questioning, and the stating is the act that the report does in the form of the naming of the excessive questioning as the thing that God dislikes. The verses address the inappropriate questioning, which is the questioning that is likely to lead to the troubling answers, and the addressing is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the naming of the state of the questioning that is the not good, and some of the commentators are careful to note that the appropriate questions, which are the questions that are asked in the genuine search for the religious guidance, are not the discouraged, and the noting is the act that the two commentators do in the form of the distinction of the two states of the questioning, since the distinction of the appropriate and the inappropriate is the act that the commentators do in the form of the keeping of the state of the question of the genuine search in the form of the thing that the verse does not forbid.

**The Pardoning and the Disclosing in the Two Readings**

The statement of the verse that if the community asks about the things while the Book is being sent down, the things will be made clear to the community, is the statement that the two commentators take in the form of the reference to the time when the messenger was still alive and able to receive the revelation, and the taking is the act that the two do in the form of the reading of the time of the sending down. The statement may indicate, in the form of the warning, that the ones who asked about such matters while the Book was still in the process of being revealed were in the particular danger of receiving the more difficult command as the result of their questions, since their questions would certainly be the answered, and the danger is the act that the two commentators state in the form of the reading of the warning. The statement can also be read as the license to ask the legitimate questions regarding what has already been revealed, for example the question of the meaning of a particular verse of the Book, and the reading as the license is the act that the commentator does in the form of the putting of the statement in the form of the permission of the asking of the question of the meaning, since the asking of the question of the meaning while the messenger is still alive to receive the revelation is the asking that the answer of the revelation discloses. The statement that God has pardoned the thing is the statement that the four commentators take in the form of the reference to the excessive or the inappropriate questioning in the past, and the taking is the act that the four do in the form of the reading of the pardoning in the matter of the past questioning. The other of the readings takes the pardoning in the reference to the past sins that might be revealed by the answers that the messenger gives to the certain inappropriate questions, and the taking is the act that the commentator does in the form of the reading of the pardoning in the matter of the sins that the answers might reveal.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:102
> Some people before you asked such questions then denied their answers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The People Before Who Asked and Disbelieved**

The verse states the state of the people before the community in the form of the asking of the similar questions and the becoming of the disbelievers, and the two acts are the acts that the verse names for the people before in the form of the sequence of the asking and the result of the disbelief. The state of the people before is the state that the verse keeps for the account of the community in the form of the warning, since the warning of the verse is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the community in the state of the awareness of the state of the people before, and the awareness of the state is the act that the verse does in the form of the reference to the asking of the questions that the people before did and the disbelief that the asking produced in the state of the people before.

**The Examples That the Book Keeps**

The Book keeps the several examples of the earlier prophets being questioned in the disingenuous or the inappropriate form by their followers, and the examples are the acts that the Book does in the form of the telling of the state of the followers of the prophets in the matter of the questioning. In the example of the sūrah of the family, the people of the law put to the prophet of the law the demand of the making of God visible to them openly, and they were seized by the thunderbolt for the wrongdoing, and the demanding and the seizing are the acts that the sūrah of the family does in the form of the telling of the state of the people of the law in the matter of the demanding of the sign. In the example of the sūrah of the family, the chiefs of the people of the law asked the prophet for the king, and when God appointed the king for them, they proceeded to question the qualifications of the king, and the asking and the questioning are the acts that the sūrah of the family does in the form of the telling of the state of the chiefs in the matter of the king. In the example of the sūrah of the family, the prophet of the law related to the people of the law the command of God to sacrifice the cow, and they responded by the series of the questions about the exact nature of the cow to be slaughtered, in the manner that suggests the inappropriate distrust of the prophet, and the series of the questions is the act that the sūrah of the family does in the form of the telling of the state of the people of the law in the matter of the cow. Like the question of the pilgrimage that the account of the verse before names, the questioning of the people made the given command the more difficult to fulfill, and the making difficult is the act that the questioning does in the form of the state of the command in the matter of the people.

**The Sign of the She-Camel**

Some of the commentators connect the reference of the verse to the earlier communities who asked the inappropriate questions of their prophets to the people who asked the prophet of the people of the well for the sign that proves that the prophet is the true prophet, and the asking for the sign is the act that the people did in the form of the demanding of the proof of the prophethood. The prophet brought them the she-camel of God as the sign for them in the state of the leaving of the she-camel to graze freely on the land of God, and the bringing of the she-camel is the act that the prophet did in the form of the giving of the sign to the people. The people cut the legs of the she-camel, and the cutting is the act that the people did in the form of the harming of the sign of God, and the people brought the destructive punishment down upon themselves by the cutting, and the bringing down of the punishment is the act that the Book does in the form of the telling of the state of the people in the matter of the sign, since the telling of the state of the people of the well in the several verses of the sūrahs of the story of the prophet and the story of the letters is the act that the Book does in the form of the repetition of the account of the sign and the harming of the sign and the punishment of the people.

**The Table of the Followers of the Prophet of the Gospel**

Some of the commentators suggest that one example of the previous peoples asking the inappropriate questions of their prophets is the followers of the prophet of the gospel who asked for the table spread with the food to be sent down to them in the verses of this sūrah, and the asking for the table is the act that the followers did in the form of the demanding of the sign of the sending down of the table. The Book does not relate the account of the table in the critical tone that the Book takes in the story of the people of the law and the cow, and the not relating in the critical tone is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the difference of the tone of the two accounts, since the tone of the account of the table is the tone that the sūrah does in the form of the statement of the granting of the table to the followers in the form of the response to the asking. The verse of the close of the account of the table warns that the one who disbelieves after God has fulfilled the request of the followers will receive the punishment that God has not inflicted on anyone of the creation before, and the warning is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the statement of the state of the one who disbelieves after the fulfillment of the request, and the state of the one is the state that the verse of the warning keeps in the form of the naming of the unprecedented punishment.

**The Verse as the Answer to the Warning of the Questioning**

The sūrah keeps this verse in the place where it answers the warning of the verse before regarding the asking of the questions whose answers may trouble the community, and the answering is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the placing of the verse of the people before after the verse of the warning. The verse of the warning is the verse that states the not asking of the community in the form of the command, and this verse is the verse that states the state of the people before in the form of the account of the asking and the disbelief, and the two together are the structure that the sūrah gives to the account of the questioning, which is the structure of the command of the not asking and the account of the state of the people before who asked and became the disbelievers held together in the two verses, and the two together are the form that the sūrah keeps for the warning of the community against the state of the questioning that the people before did in the form of the act that led them to the disbelief.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:103

> Allah has never ordained the ˹so-called˺ baḥîrah, sâ'ibah, waṣîlah, and ḥâm camels. But the disbelievers just fabricate lies about Allah, and most of them lack understanding.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Four Terms in the Verse**

The verse keeps four Arabic terms in the state of the naming, and the four terms name the four kinds of camels that the idolaters of the land used to set apart as the sacred and to dedicate to the gods of the land. The camel that was so set apart was not put to the riding or to the milking for the use of the people, and the people let the animal roam and feed without the putting to any use. The scholars of the commentaries do not keep the exact agreement on the nature of each of the four kinds, and the differences that they point out among the kinds pass into one another, so that the account of one kind is kept in part in the state of the account of the other kind.

**The Account of the Baḥīrah and the Sāʾibah**

Some of the accounts are that the baḥīrah was the she-camel at the birth of her fifth young, and the fifth was, in some of the accounts, the male of the five. The sāʾibah was, in the reading that is kept by most of the accounts, the she-camel that the owner dedicated to the gods of his own accord as the gift of the supplication for the overcoming of an illness, or as the protection on the journey, or as the giving of the thanks for the good fortune that came to the owner. In the other reading, the word was taken in the meaning of the slave who was set free in the full sense, in the state in which every tie to the former master was cut, and the taking of the word in the meaning of the person is the reading that keeps the account of the term apart from the account of the animal.

**The Account of the Waṣīlah and the Ḥām**

The waṣīlah was, in one of the readings, the she-camel who had given many daughters, and the daughters were of the special value for the milk of the family. In the other reading, the waṣīlah was the she-camel who had a male and a female of the young. The ḥām is the only one of the four terms that names the male of the camels, and the account is that he was the male who had sired the many of the young, or that his young had in their turn sired another generation, and the animal was let to graze in the free state and was not put to the riding or to the carrying of any load.

**The Mark of the Split Ear**

The camel that was so set apart was given, in many of the accounts, the mark of the split ear, so that the one who came upon the animal knew the state of the sacredness and did not trouble it. The sūrah of the Cow keeps the account of the slitting of the ear of the cattle in the verse in which Satan promises to mislead the people, and the commanding of the slitting of the ear is among the things that Satan takes as the way of the misleading. The verses of the sūrah al-Anʿām keep the criticism of the setting apart, of their own authority, of particular kinds of the cattle as the sacred, and the verse of the sūrah Yūnus keeps the questioning of the ones who made some of the provision of God lawful and some unlawful without the authority of God.

**The First Turning From the Religion of Abraham**

These acts of the setting apart are reported by the commentators to be among the first ways in which the Arabs of the time before the religion turned from the monotheism of Abraham, and the naming of Abraham as the origin of the faith of the Arabs is the reading that keeps the account of the turning in the state of the leaving of the known faith. Besides the state of the idolatry, the acts also carry the violation of the repeated objection of the sūrah to the restricting, of the own authority, of the enjoyment of the good things that God has made for the people, and the objection is kept in the verses of the sūrah that follow in the state of the declaration of the lawful of the pure and the good.

**The Fabrication Against God**

The verse opens in the state of the declaration that God did not put these four kinds in the state of the established, and the declaration is the form that the sūrah does in the denial of the origin of the four practices from God. The sūrah does not keep the four kinds of camels in the state of the law of God, and the attribution of the sacredness of the four kinds to God is, in the state of the verse, the fabrication of the lie against God. The fabrication of the lie against God is the enormous sin that the sūrah connects often with the innovations of the religion that the previous communities took for the law of God, and the similar statements of the fabrication are kept in the sūrah of the Family, in the sūrah al-Anʿām, in the sūrah al-Aʿrāf, in the sūrah Yūnus, and in the sūrah al-Naḥl. The verse closes in the state of the statement that most of the ones who disbelieve do not understand, and the closing is the reading that keeps the account of the disbeliever in the state of the not understanding of what he has taken as the law of the gods.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:104

> When it is said to them, “Come to Allah’s revelations and to the Messenger,” they reply, “What we found our forefathers practicing is good enough for us.” ˹Would they still do so,˺ even if their forefathers had absolutely no knowledge or guidance?

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Verse of the Cow That Stands Beside It**

The verse of the sūrah of the Cow that stands beside the present one is the verse in which the people are told to follow the revelation of God, and the answer of the people is the keeping of the practice of the forefathers, even in the state in which the forefathers carried no understanding and no guidance. The present verse follows the verse of the sūrah that turned aside the act of the setting apart of certain camels as the sacred without the authority, and the present verse does in the form of the suggestion that such an act cannot be kept in the state of the justified by the claim of the one who follows the tradition of the fathers.

**The Theme of the Refusal of the Prophets**

The sūrah keeps, in many of the narratives of the prophets, the account of the peoples who turned away from the message that was sent to them, and the turning away of the peoples was in the state of the unwillingness to leave the acts of the idolatry and the acts of the unrighteousness that were with the peoples. The people took the fathers and the corrupt guidance of the fathers as the ground of the turning away from the prophet, and some of the people showed the anger at the demand of the prophet that they leave the practice of the fathers. The verse of the sūrah Sabaʾ keeps the account in which the people say, at the recitation of the clear revelation, that the prophet is only the one who wishes to turn them from the worship of the forefathers, and the similar statements are kept in the sūrah of the Cow, in the sūrah al-Aʿrāf that asks the people whether the prophet has come to them so that they would worship God alone and leave the worship of the forefathers, in the sūrah Hūd, in the sūrah Ibrāhīm, in the sūrah al-Muʾminūn, in the sūrah al-Qaṣaṣ, in the sūrah Luqmān, and in the sūrah al-Zukhruf.

**The Fathers as the Excuse from the Blame**

In the other places of the sūrah, the disbelievers take the teaching of the fathers as the excuse for the acts of the idolatry and the acts of the unrighteousness that are their own, and the excuse does not carry the deflecting of the blame from the people. The verse of the sūrah al-Aʿrāf keeps the account in which the people say, at the committing of the shameful act, that they found the forefathers in the act and that God commanded them to the act, and the answer of the sūrah is the declaration that God does not command the shameful. The sūrah al-Isrāʾ and the sūrah ash-Shuʿarāʾ keep the similar statement in the narrative of Abraham, who calls his people to the questioning of the following of the teaching of the fathers, and the naming of Abraham is the reading that keeps the account of the questioning of the inheritance in the state of the example of the prophet.

**The Ground of the Refusal of the Blind Following**

This theme, which the sūrah keeps in many of the narratives of the prophets, is the ground on which the scholars rest the refusal of the handing over of the own judgment to the one who is held to be the authority in the doctrine of the religion, without the inquiry, and the reading of the commentators takes the verse as the ground of the refusal in the form of the extension of the sense of the verse to the state of the religious authority. At the very least, the verse does in the form of the showing that the one is the responsible for the finding of the worthy source of the instruction and of the guidance in the religion, for the disbelievers of the verse are called to the rebuke at the following of the ones who knew naught and who were not in the state of the guidance, and the rebuke is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the question at the end of the verse.

**The Personal Responsibility of the Individual**

The sūrah does not keep the responsibility of the person in the state of the custom that is inherited, and the individual is, in the state of the sūrah, the morally responsible for the choice of the own religion on the ground of the inborn knowledge of the existence of God and of His being one, which the tradition of the Islam takes as the awareness that is in the person from the origin. The sūrah says that the people testified to this awareness in the covenant that was before the time, as the verse of the sūrah al-Aʿrāf has related. The question that the sūrah keeps at the end of the verse, in which the state of the knowing naught of the fathers is put forward, is the reading that does in the form of the showing of the weakness of the claim of the following of the fathers, because the following of the one who knows naught does not carry the state of the guidance to the one who follows, and the sūrah leaves the person in the state of the responsibility of the inquiry into what he is called to follow.

**The Two Things to Which the People Are Called**

The sūrah keeps the call of the present verse in the form of the two things to which the people are called, and the two things are the revelation of God and the messenger. The pairing of the revelation and of the messenger is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the call, and the verse of the sūrah of the Family that keeps the command of the obedience to God and to His messenger keeps the similar pairing in the state of the command. The sūrah, in the present verse, does not keep the call in the state of the command to the obedience of the two, but it keeps it in the state of the calling of the people to come to the two, and the coming to the two is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the invitation rather than the command. The answer of the people, that the practice of the forefathers is enough for them, is the reading that the sūrah keeps in the form of the refusal of the coming to the two, and the sūrah shows the weakness of the refusal in the state of the question of the end of the verse.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:105

> O believers! You are accountable only for yourselves. It will not harm you if someone chooses to deviate—as long as you are ˹rightly˺ guided. To Allah you will all return, and He will inform you of what you used to do.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Charge of the Own Soul**

The previous verse turned aside the claim of the disbelievers who kept the act of the fathers as the ground of the refusal of the guidance of the prophet, and the present verse is read, in one of the senses, as the assertion that every human being has the charge of the fate of the own soul. The expression of the verse is read, in the reading that some of the commentators keep, in the state of the imperative, as the call of the people to the taking of the charge of the own soul, and the call is the admonition to the keeping of the own soul from the disobedience to the religion, and the taking of the expression in the imperative is the reading that does in the form of the calling of the person to the state of the preservation.

**The Reference of the One Who Is Astray**

The expression of the verse that the one deviates is the reference that may be kept in the state of the fathers from whom the disbelievers of the previous verse claimed the taking of the religious guidance. The reference may be kept in the state of the other disbelievers, or in the state of Satan. The sūrah keeps, in several of the verses, the account of Satan in the state of the not possessing of the independent authority over the souls of the people or over the choices of the morality of the people. The verse of the sūrah Ibrāhīm keeps the declaration of Satan in which he says that he had no authority over the people and that he only called them and they answered to him. The similar statements are kept in the verse of the sūrah al-Hijr that says that he will have no authority over the servants of God except the deviant who follow him, in the verse of the sūrah an-Naḥl that says that he has no authority over the ones who believe and put the trust in their Lord, in the verse of the sūrah al-Isrāʾ, and in the verse of the sūrah Sabaʾ, and the naming of these verses is the reading that keeps the account of the one who deviates in the state of the no authority over the one who is guided.

**The Relation to the Forbidding of the Wrong**

Most of the commentators take the verse in the meaning that the one is, in the end, the morally responsible for the one-self, and the main concern of the commentators with the verse was the extent to which the idea of the responsibility of the one-self would touch the weight of the enjoining of the right and the forbidding of the wrong. This principle is established for the believers in the other verses of the sūrah, as the verse of the sūrah of the Family that keeps the statement that there is to be among the people the group that calls the others to the good and encourages the good and forbids the wrong, and the principle is held as one of the religious obligations of all the Muslims, in the state of the individual and in the state of the community. Most of the commentators keep, therefore, the reading that the verse does not cancel the obligation of the enjoining of the right and the forbidding of the wrong, but that it does in the form of the showing that, if the call of the one does not get the hearing in the end, the one does not carry the moral responsibility for the ones who refused to take the admonition.

**The Other Readings of the Charge**

The others take the expression of the charge of the own soul in the meaning that the one has the charge over the ones of the own, in the sense that the one has the moral responsibility to guide the members of the own community of the religion, and not the ones who are outside of it. Some of the commentators say that the meaning that is intended is that the one is not to be in the state of the grieving over the stubbornness of the others at the face of the moral admonition, since even the prophet is told in the sūrah that he has no control over the accepting or the not accepting of the message of the Divine that he brings, and the naming of the account of the prophet is the reading that keeps the person in the state of the not grieving over the one who refuses. The various readings, taken together, keep the sense of the verse in the state of the responsibility of the own soul while they do not cancel the obligation of the calling of the others to the good.

**The Time of the Application of the Verse**

Some of the statements that are carried to the Companions of the prophet keep the reading that the time of the effect of the message of the verse had not yet come, and the suggestion of the statements was that the state in which the people become responsible for the keeping of the own soul from the destruction of the morality is the state of the distant future, which may be the future of the end of the time, in which the people are far from the taking of the guidance of the religion and the world is filled with the enormities of the morality. This is the reading that is weak, however, since it keeps the relevance of the verse in the state of the circumstances of the distant future, and the sūrah does not keep the application of the verse in the state of the waiting for the time.

**The Address and the Informing of God**

The sūrah opens the verse with the address to the believers, and the address is the form that the sūrah does in the state of the calling of the community to the charge of the own soul after the account of the disbelievers and of the fathers of the disbelievers in the previous verses. The idea that God will inform the people in the Hereafter about the deeds of the past of the people and the matters in which there was the difference of the religion is mentioned in several other places of the sūrah, as the verse of the present sūrah that speaks of the informing of God in the state of the return of the people all together to Him keeps the statement in the form of the declaration of the informing.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:106

> O believers! When death approaches any of you, call upon two just Muslim men to witness as you make a bequest; otherwise, two nonMuslims if you are afflicted with death while on a journey. If you doubt ˹their testimony˺, keep them after prayer and let them testify under oath ˹saying˺, “By Allah! We would never sell our testimony for any price, even in favour of a close relative, nor withhold the testimony of Allah. Otherwise, we would surely be sinful.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Bequest and the Measure of It**

The law of the Islam allows the one, before the coming of the death, to leave up to one third of the wealth to any one who is not already in the state of the entitled to the ordained share of the inheritance, and the ordained shares are put down in the verses of the sūrah of the Cow in the form of the assignment of the specific shares to the parents, the widows, and the other close relatives, and the verses keep the statement that the male is entitled to twice the share of the female and that each parent has a share after the fulfilling of the bequests and the debts. The sūrah commanded, in the first place, the leaving of the bequests in the state of the general, for the parents and the other relatives, and the verse of the sūrah of the Cow keeps the statement that, when the death comes near to any of the people, the will is to be made in the favour of the parents and the immediate family with the fairness. The verse of the sūrah of the Cow that follows assigns to the widow a year of the maintenance, and the verse of the bequest of the widow is the reading that keeps the account of the bequest before the verse of the shares. After the revelation of the verses of the shares, most of the schools of the law of the Islam took the heirs who are stipulated in the state of the not eligible to receive such a bequest, but one of the stipulations of the verses of the shares is that the bequests that are known as the made by the deceased to the others are to be given effect before the division of the rest of the property among the heirs of the law.

**The Two Witnesses in the Presence of the Death**

The bequest of this kind is to be made in the state of the presence of the two witnesses, and this is also true of the other transactions of the property, as the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that concerns the contracting of the debt keeps in the state of the calling upon two men to witness, and as the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that concerns the giving of the property of the orphans to them at the reaching of the maturity keeps in the state of the calling in the witnesses. The witness that is between the people, in the time when the death comes near, is, in the present verse, to be the two men of the justice who are among you, and the two men are taken by many of the commentators in the meaning of the two men who are from among the community of the Muslims. The two who are from among the other than the people could thus refer to the non-Muslims who might be called to the witnessing of the bequest at the time when the one is in the travel upon the earth, in the state that is presumably among the non-Muslims. Some of the commentators take the non-Muslim witnesses in the state of the being from any community of the religion, since the verse does not keep the specificity on this matter, but the others take them in the state of the being from the community of the Jews, the Christians, or the Zoroastrians, and the testimony of the ones who belong to these three communities has been accepted in the law of the courts of the Islam. In the courts of the law of the Islam in India, the testimony of the Hindus was also accepted in some of the cases. A minority takes the two men of the justice among you in the meaning of the two men from among the neighbors of the one, in which case the two who are from among the other than the people would refer to the ones who are outside of the immediate community of the one. The verse is understood, on the ground of the gender of the adjective that carries the meaning of the justice, as the requiring of the two male witnesses.

**The Occasion the Reports Keep**

The present verse was, in the report of the commentators, revealed after the incident that involved a man of the faith who fell sick on a journey kept by two companions who were Christians and who died there, and the report is kept in the state of the account of the occasion of the verse. The young man put the possessions of his in the charge of the companions to give them to the family of his, and upon the return the companions gave the possessions of the man to the family of the man, and the prophet had the two of them take the oath that nothing had been held back. In the time after, however, the state came to be known in which the two had kept for themselves a drinking vessel of the silver that was of the value, and when one of the heirs of the deceased saw the cup he knew it as one of the possessions of the deceased man and testified that it was to be part of the bequest. The sūrah does not keep the report in the text of the verse, but the commentators place the report in the state of the occasion of the verse, and the placing is the reading that keeps the connection of the verse to the state of the travel and of the witnessing among the people of the other faiths.

**The Detaining After the Prayer**

The commentators keep generally the reading that the detaining of the witnesses, or the making of the two to swear the oath before God, is the needed only in the cases in which the faithfulness of the witnesses is in the state of the suspicion, and the taking of the detaining as the act of the state of the suspicion is the reading that keeps the procedure in the form of the precaution. The detaining of the witnesses after the prayer is taken by many in the meaning of the after of the prayer of the late afternoon of the Islam, and the report was that the practice of the prophet was the exacting of the oaths from the people at this time in the specific state. Some of the commentators keep, however, the maintenance that the prayer does not necessarily refer to the canonical prayer of the Islam, for the witnesses might be the non-Muslims in the state of the account, in which case they are to be detained after the prayer of their own, and the taking is on the ground that the one who has completed the prayer is more in the state of the faithfulness of the witness and of the fear of the making of the false oath before God. The oath not to sell the witness for the price is the promise that the witnesses do not profit from the witnessing of their own in any way, and the promise is kept in the form of the words of the oath that the sūrah places in the mouth of the witnesses.

**The Oath Sworn by God**

The verse is also the evidence that the solemn oaths are to be sworn by God, and the idea is supported by the hadith that says that the one who would swear an oath is to swear by God or to be in the state of the silence. The scholar al-Shāfiʿī, who is the foundational of the legal scholars, however, also allowed the swearing upon a written copy of the sūrah. The significance of the taking of the oath is kept in the verse of the present sūrah that addresses the oaths of the people and the atonement of the one who swears, and the connection of the two verses is the reading that keeps the account of the oath in the state of the solemnity of the witnessing. The sūrah, in the present verse, keeps the procedure of the bequest in the state of the protection of the property of the deceased from the holding back of the witnesses, and the verse is the reading that does in the form of the setting of the two witnesses and of the oath that the two are to take before God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:107

> If they are found guilty ˹of false testimony˺, let the deceased’s two closest heirs affected by the bequest replace the witnesses and testify under oath ˹saying˺, “By Allah! Our testimony is truer than theirs. We have not transgressed. Otherwise, we would surely be wrongdoers.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Discovery of the Sin of the Two**

The expression of the verse that the two are found guilty of the sin refers to the two witnesses of the previous verse being found to have betrayed the trust that they undertook in the specific state as the witnesses of the bequest of the deceased, and the taking of the sin as the betrayal of the trust is the reading that the commentators keep in the form of the explanation of the state of the guilt. The sūrah says that, if the discovery is that the two are in the state of the guilty of the sin, then the two others stand in the place of the two, and the standing in the place is the act that the sūrah does in the form of the providing of the replacement of the witnesses in the state of the discovery of the unfaithfulness. The verse keeps the procedure of the bequest in the state of the continuation of the protection of the property of the deceased after the failure of the first witnesses.

**The Two Others in the State of the Claim**

The two others who stand in the place of the first witnesses are, in the state of the verse, among the ones who carry the greatest claim upon the bequest that the deceased has left, and the rights of the claimants are the ones that the unfaithful witnesses have therefore violated. The phrase that the verse keeps as the claimants is taken, in the other rendering, in the meaning of the ones who are nearest to it, and the nearest of the ones is the one who is most entitled to the receiving of the bequest, and it is also the one who is nearest to the affairs of the deceased, and the nearness to the affairs carries the greater knowledge of the state of the deceased, and the taking of the phrase in the second meaning is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the extension of the sense of the nearness from the state of the claim to the state of the knowledge of the affairs.

**The Oath of the Two Others**

The two others are to take the oath to the truthfulness of their own because they are the claimants of the bequest and not the witnesses who carry no interest in it, and the requiring of the oath of the claimants is the reading that keeps the procedure in the state of the showing of the state of the interest of the two others. As part of this oath, the two swear that their witness is truer than the witness of the first two, and they swear that they have not transgressed, and the transgressing is, in the explanation of the commentators, the exceeding of the limits in the witness that the two have given in the matter of the bequest. The form of the oath of the two others is kept in the verse in the state of the parallel of the oath of the first two witnesses, and the sūrah places the words of the oath in the mouth of the two others in the way that it placed the words of the oath in the mouth of the first two in the previous verse.

**The Verse in the Account of the Bequest**

The verse stands between the verse of the first two witnesses and the verse that speaks of the likelihood of the bearing of the true witness, and the three verses together keep the account of the bequest in the state of the procedure that the sūrah sets out for the community. The present verse is the middle of the three, and it does in the form of the providing of the case of the discovery of the sin of the first witnesses and of the replacement of the two by the two claimants. The sūrah keeps the procedure in the state of the completeness of the protection, so that the bequest of the deceased is not left in the state of the exposure to the holding back of the unfaithful witnesses.

**The Two Oaths in the Parallel**

The sūrah, in the two verses of the witnesses, keeps the two oaths in the state of the parallel, and the form of the parallel is the reading that the sūrah does in the account of the bequest. The first oath is the oath of the two witnesses that they will not sell the witness for any price and will not conceal the witness of God, and the second oath is the oath of the two claimants that their witness is truer than the witness of the first two and that they have not transgressed. The two oaths, in the state of the parallel, keep the account of the bequest in the form of the solemnity of the witnessing, and the sūrah leaves the property of the deceased in the state of the protection from the two states of the unfaithfulness of the witnesses and of the transgression of the claimants.

**The Protection of the Trust in the Sūrah**

The sūrah of the Cow keeps the command to return the trusts to their rightful owners and to judge between the people with fairness, and the verse of the sūrah of the Cow is the reading that keeps the account of the witnesses of the bequest in the state of the protection of the trust. The property of the deceased that the witnesses bear is a trust in the state of the bequest, and the sūrah keeps the trust in the state of the protection from the holding back of the unfaithful witnesses in the form of the procedure of the two verses of the witnesses. The verse of the sūrah al-Anfāl that says that the people are not to betray the trusts knowingly keeps the similar statement in the state of the prohibition of the betrayal, and the naming of the verse is the reading that keeps the account of the witnesses in the form of the obligation of the faithful bearing of the trust. The sūrah, in the present verse, completes the account of the discovery of the sin of the first witnesses and of the replacement of the two by the two claimants, and it leaves the trust of the bequest in the state of the protection from the two states of the unfaithfulness and of the transgression.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:108

> In this way it is more likely that witnesses will give true testimony or else fear that their oaths could be refuted by those of the heirs. Be mindful of Allah and obey. For Allah does not guide the rebellious people.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Purpose of the Procedures**

The procedures that the two previous verses of the sūrah set out for the assuring of the truthful witness of the ones who are present at the bequest are, in the state of the account, meant to put the witnesses in the state in which the executing of the bequest is faithful, and they do also in the form of the increasing of the trust of the people in the matters of this kind. The sūrah keeps the procedures in the state of the protection of the bequest of the deceased from the unfaithfulness of the witnesses, and the taking of the purpose of the procedures as the protection is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the sense of the verses. The present verse gathers the purpose of the two previous verses in the state of the single statement, and the statement is kept in the form of the expression that the two are more likely to bear the true witness.

**The Imprinting of the Solemnity of the Oath**

Some of the commentators take the verses in the meaning that they are meant to put upon all the people the awareness of the solemnity and the weight of the taking of the oaths and of the serving as the witnesses to such bequests, and the taking of the verses as the imprinting of the solemnity is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the extension of the sense of the procedures from the state of the bequest to the state of the oath in general. The verse of the present sūrah that addresses the oaths of the people and the atonement of the one who swears keeps the similar statement in the state of the calling of the people to the mindfulness of the oaths, and the connection of the present verse to that verse is the reading that keeps the account of the oath in the form of the solemnity. The three verses of the bequest keep the oath in the state of the weight of the matter before God.

**The Duty of the Truthful Witness**

The three verses of the bequest stand, in the state of the account, among the passages of the sūrah that keep the insistence upon the religious duty of the truthful witness, and the duty of the truthful witness is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that says that the people are to stand firm for God and bear true testimony, and are not to let the hatred of a people lead them to injustice. The passages that keep the insistence upon the duty of the faithful fulfilling of the matters with which the one has been entrusted are in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that speaks of the fulfilling of the trusts, in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that commands the returning of the trusts to their rightful owners, in the verse of the sūrah al-Anfāl that says that the people are not to betray the trusts knowingly, in the verses of the sūrah al-Muʾminūn that keep the description of the believers as the ones who are true to their trusts and covenants, and in the verses of the sūrah ʿAbasa that keep the similar description of the faithful. The naming of these verses is the reading that keeps the account of the witnesses of the bequest in the state of the general duty of the truthful witness and of the faithful bearing of the trust.

**The Fear of the Refuting of the Oaths**

The sūrah says, moreover, that the second purpose of the procedures is the state in which the two fear the meeting of the oaths of their own by the oaths that come after, and the meeting of the oaths is the state in which the oath of the one witness is met by the oath of the other. The sūrah keeps the state in the form of the provision that the sūrah makes for the case of the suspicion of the unfaithfulness. The witnesses, in the fear of the meeting of the oaths, are kept in the state of the caution in the bearing of the witness, and the sūrah does in the form of the protection of the bequest from the state of the refuting of the oaths. The two purposes that the present verse keeps complete the account of the procedures of the witnesses of the bequest.

**The Closing of the Verse and of the Account**

The sūrah closes the verse with the calling of the people to the reverence of God and to the obeying, and the closing is the form that the sūrah does in the state of the address to the community after the account of the procedures. The expression that God guides not the rebellious people is the reading that the sūrah keeps in the state of the declaration of the state of the one who is not guided, and the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the condition of the guidance. The three verses of the bequest keep the account of the bequest in the state of the completeness of the procedure, and it leaves the community in the state of the charge of the truthful witness and of the faithful bearing of the trust. The sūrah does in the present verse in the form of the closing of the account of the bequest, and the verse is the reading that keeps the procedures of the two previous verses in the state of the purpose and of the solemnity of the oath.

**The Command of the Listening and the Obeying**

The sūrah keeps the command of the obeying in the state of the address to the community, and the verse of the sūrah al-Aʿrāf that says that, when the sūrah is recited, the people are to listen to it attentively and be silent so they may be shown mercy keeps the similar command in the form of the calling of the people to the receiving of the recitation. The present verse does in the form of the pairing of the two commands, the reverence of God and the obeying, and the pairing is the reading that keeps the closing of the account of the bequest in the state of the calling of the community to the state of the mindfulness and of the attention.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:109

> ˹Consider˺ the Day Allah will gather the messengers and say, “What response did you receive?” They will reply, “We have no knowledge ˹compared to You˺! You ˹alone˺ are indeed the Knower of all unseen.”

**The Day Kept in the Form of the Warning**

This verse is the warning that the sūrah keeps in the state of the day in which God gathers the messengers, and the verse can be taken in the meaning of the caution of the people about the day, even though the sūrah does not keep an explicit warning in the text of the verse. Some of the commentators take the verse as the following upon the admonition of the previous verse, in which the people are called to the reverence of God and to the listening, and the others take it as the following upon the last sentence of the previous verse, in which the sūrah keeps the declaration of the not guiding of the iniquitous people, so that the day is read in the state of the day in which God guides not the iniquitous people, and the reading of the verse as the continuation of the admonition is the form that the commentators do in the account of the sequence of the two verses.

**The Question Kept as the Rebuke of the Community**

The question that God keeps in the verse to the messengers is, in the reading of some of the commentators, the rebuke that is kept for the community of the messengers, and the taking of the question as the rebuke of the community is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the extension of the sense of the question from the mouth of the messengers to the state of their community. The question is addressed to the messengers because they are the leaders of the community, and the naming of the leadership of the messengers is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the address of the question to the messengers rather than to the community directly.

**The Claim of the Not Knowing**

The messengers, in the answer that they keep in the verse, claim the not knowing of the matter, and the claim is the reading that the sūrah does in the mouth of the messengers after the question of God. Some of the commentators suggest that the messengers, on the day, will be too much in the state of the awe of the terror of the day to give the answer at once, and that they will in the time after regain the composure and proceed to the bearing of the witness to the actions of their respective community, and this explanation is hard to keep in the state of the harmony with the other verses of the sūrah that keep the statement that the believers, and the messengers are surely among them, are not in the state of the fear on the day. The similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that says that there will be no fear for the believers and no grief, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Anbiyāʾ that says that the Supreme Horror will not disturb them and that the angels will greet them with the day that has been promised to them.

**The Other Reading of the Claim**

The answer that the messengers keep, in which they put the knowledge of the unseen in the state of the knowledge of God alone, may be the indication of the state in which the messengers are not the aware of what their community has done in the time after their own passing, or of the secret intentions of their followers, and the taking of the claim in this reading is the form that the commentators do in the state of the explanation of the not knowing. Yet this explanation also seems not to be in the state of the harmony with the idea of the sūrah that the messengers bear the witness against their community on the day of the judgment. The similar statements are kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that speaks of the making of the community as the upright one for the witnessing over humanity and of the messenger as the witness over them, in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the statement that the one who obeys God and the messenger is in the company of the prophets and the people of the truth, in the verse of the sūrah al-Hajj that says that the messenger is to be the witness over the people who submit, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Ḥadīd that keeps the statement of the people of the truth in the company of God.

**The Humility Before the Knowledge of God**

The claim of the not knowing of the messengers is, in the reading that some of the commentators keep as the best, the expression of the humility of the messengers before the knowledge of God, and the expression is the form that the messengers do in the state of the putting of their own knowledge under the knowledge of God. The reading is that the messengers say, in effect, that they have no knowledge save what God has taught them, and this is the same as the answer that the angels give to God in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow in which the angels glorify God and declare that they have no knowledge except what He has taught them. The other reading is that the messengers say that they have no knowledge save that the knowledge of God of the matter is the same or greater, and the taking of the claim in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the humility. In the same vein, the claim may also be the acknowledgment of the messengers that, although they know the outward aspects of the actions of the people, God alone knows the secrets of the people. The messengers, although they are aware of the deeds of their community, prefer to entrust the matter of the judgment of their community to God, and the entrusting is the act that the messengers do in the form of the putting of the judgment in the hand of God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:110

> And ˹on Judgment Day˺ Allah will say, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Remember My favour upon you and your mother: how I supported you with the holy spirit so you spoke to people in ˹your˺ infancy and adulthood. How I taught you writing, wisdom, the Torah, and the Gospel. How you moulded a bird from clay—by My Will—and breathed into it and it became a ˹real˺ bird—by My Will. How you healed the blind and the lepers—by My Will. How you brought the dead to life—by My Will. How I prevented the Children of Israel from harming you when you came to them with clear proofs and the disbelievers among them said, “This is nothing but pure magic.”

**The Address of God to Jesus on the Day**

This verse continues in the context of the sūrah in which God speaks to the messengers on the day of the judgment, and from the present verse and for nearly the remainder of the sūrah, God addresses Jesus in the specific state regarding his mission and the actions of his followers, and the address of Jesus is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the day. The present verse rehearses, in the mouth of God, the many of the extraordinary qualities that the sūrah attributes to Jesus in the other verses, and the rehearsal is the act that the sūrah does in the state of the recollection of the favor upon Jesus and upon his mother.

**The Support with the Holy Spirit**

Jesus is, in the present verse, described as the one whom God supported with the holy spirit, and the similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that gives Jesus the clear proofs and supports him with the holy spirit, and in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the similar statement in the account of the choosing of some of the messengers above the others. The role of the spirit in the creation of Jesus is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that says that Jesus is a spirit from God, in the verse of the sūrah Meryam that sends the angel to Mary in the state of her screening from the people, in the verse of the sūrah al-Anbiyāʾ that breathes into Mary through the angel so that she and her son become a sign for all the peoples, and in the verse of the sūrah at-Ṭāhr that keeps the similar statement in the account of the guarded chastity of Mary. The holy spirit is taken by many, in its context of the sūrah and in the tradition of the commentary, as the reference to the archangel Gabriel, the angel of the revelation, and the similar statements are kept in the verse of the sūrah an-Naḥl that says that the holy spirit brings the revelation down from the Lord with the truth, in the verse of the sūrah al-Isrāʾ in which the people ask about the spirit and the answer is that its nature is known only to the Lord, in the verse of the sūrah ash-Shuʿarāʾ, in the verse of the sūrah ash-Shūrā, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Qadr that keeps the statement of the descending of the angels and the spirit on the night of the decree by the permission of their Lord. The spirit is also identified with the spirit as such, which the sūrah describes as the one that is from the command of the Lord, and the grammatical form of the phrase is taken by some in the rendering of the spirit of the holy, or of the spirit of God, since it is related to the name of God that keeps the state of the holiness, as the verse of the sūrah al-Ḥashr that keeps the names of God including the Most Holy keeps the similar statement. Given the connection between Jesus and the spirit, the prophetic title of Jesus is the spirit of God, and the title can be used, in the state of the account, as a proper name in the Islam.

**The Teaching of the Book and the Wisdom**

Jesus is also described, in the present verse, as the one to whom God taught the writing, the wisdom, the Torah, and the Gospel, and the description is kept in the form of the parallel of the other prophets. The verse of the sūrah of the Family keeps the similar statement in the covenant that God made with the prophets, in which He says that He has given them the book and the wisdom and that they are to believe in and support the messenger who comes after them confirming what is with them. The verse of the sūrah of the Cow says that God has given the descendants of Abraham the book and the wisdom along with the great authority, and the phrase of the book and the wisdom is used, in the reading of the commentators, to refer to the scriptures of the prophets and to the knowledge of the prophets of their meaning. The similar statements are kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the prayer of the raising of the messenger who teaches the book and the wisdom, in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that keeps the favor of God in the raising of the messenger who recites the revelations and teaches the book and the wisdom, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Jumuʿa that keeps the similar statement in the account of the messenger who is raised for the people who were previously in clear error. The present verse also says that God gave Jesus the clear proofs, and the clear proofs are said of Jesus in the similar state in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow, and the term is used throughout the sūrah as the general word for the revelation of God in all of its forms.

**The Miracles that the Verse Rehearses**

The verse keeps, in the rehearsal of the favor, the account of the speaking of Jesus to the people in the state of the infancy, and the similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that says that he will speak to the people in the state of the infancy and the adulthood, and in the verse of the sūrah Meryam in which the people are surprised at the talking with the infant in the cradle, and the speaking in the state of the infancy is the attribute that is kept in the sūrah and not found in the Bible. The verse also rehearses the making of the bird out of the clay, the breathing into it so that it becomes the bird, the healing of the blind and the leper, and the bringing of the dead to the life, and the similar account is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family in which Jesus speaks of these powers to the people of the Children of Israel in the state of the sign from their Lord. These last three powers are extraordinary, in the state of the account, in that they suggest the powers that are usually reserved for God, since the healing of the blind and the leper is kept in the sūrah as the act done by the permission of God, and the raising of the dead is the act of God throughout the sūrah, and the creation of the human being out of the clay and the breathing of the spirit into it is kept in the verse of the sūrah al-Hijr that creates man from the sounding clay moulded from the black mud, in the verse of the sūrah as-Sajda that originates the creation of the human being from the clay and fashions it and has the spirit breathed into it, and in the verse of the sūrah aṣ-āḍāt that keeps the account of the creation of the human being from the clay. The verse, however, makes clear in the repeated statement that all of these powers are done by the leave of God, and the powers are, in the end, the powers of God. The healing and the raising of the dead are also kept in the books of the Gospels, and a similar account of the making of the bird from the clay is kept in the account of the infancy that is not in the canon of the Gospels.

**The Restraint of the Children of Israel**

The verse keeps, at the end of the rehearsal, the statement that God prevented the Children of Israel from harming Jesus in the time when he came to them with the clear proofs, and the preventing is, in the reading of most of the commentators, the meaning of the thwarting of the attempt of the people to slay Jesus, and the sūrah asserts in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that the people did not slay Jesus and did not crucify him, and the verse of the sūrah of the Cow keeps the statement that it was only made to appear so to them. The accusation of the sorcery that the disbelievers among the Children of Israel keep in the present verse is kept in the similar state in the verse of the sūrah aṣ-Ṣaff that says that the people said of the prophet, when he came to them with the clear proofs, that this is pure magic. The similar accusations are kept against the messengers of God in general in the verse of the sūrah al-Anʿām that says that the disbelievers said of the truth that it is pure magic, in the verse of the sūrah Yūnus that keeps the similar statement, in the verse of the sūrah al-Anbiyāʾ, in the verse of the sūrah al-Zukhruf, in the verse of the sūrah al-Aḥqāf, in the verse of the sūrah al-Ḥāqqah, and in the verse of the sūrah that speaks of the turning away from the sign with the word of the magic. The similar accusations are also kept against Moses in the verse of the sūrah Yūnus, in the verse of the sūrah Ṭā-Hā, in the verse of the sūrah an-Naml, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Qaṣa, and against the messengers of God in the verse of the sūrah Hūd, in the verse of the sūrah Sabaʾ, and in the verse of the sūrah aṣ-Ṣāḍāt.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:111

> And how I inspired the disciples, “Believe in Me and My messenger!” They declared, “We believe and bear witness that we fully submit ˹to Allah˺.”

**The Table That Gives the Sūrah Its Name**

The verses of the sūrah that keep this account recount the incident in which the disciples of Jesus request the table that is spread with the food, and God sends it down from the heaven, and the table is the thing from which the sūrah takes its name, and the naming of the sūrah by the table is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the title of the sūrah.
