The present verse keeps the statement of the inspiring of the disciples, and the statement is the form that the sūrah does in the mouth of God speaking in the first person, and the keeping of the inspiring in the mouth of God is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the origin of the belief of the disciples.

**The Testimony of the Disciples**

The disciples, in the answer that they keep in the verse, declare the belief and the bearing of the witness that they are the submitters to God, and the similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family in which the disciples answer the question of Jesus with the declaration that they will stand for God and that they believe in God and bear the witness that they have submitted. The verse of the sūrah of the Family that follows keeps the statement that the disbelievers made the plan against Jesus and that God also made the plan, and that God is the best of the planners, and the naming of the plan is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the contrast of the plan of the disbelievers and the plan of God.

**The Inspiring in the Form of the Casting into the Heart**

God speaks in the first person in the present verse to state that He inspired the disciples to the belief in Him and in His messenger, and the verb that the sūrah keeps for the inspiring is the one that is related to the word that usually denotes the revelation of the prophet, and the verb is, in this context, taken in the gloss of the casting into the heart, and the casting into the heart is the form of the inspiring that is less direct and less in the state of the verbatim than the form that the prophets received. The taking of the verb in this gloss is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the distinction of the inspiring of the disciples from the inspiring of the prophets, and the distinction is the reading that keeps the inspiring of the disciples in the state of the inspiration of the heart rather than in the state of the revelation of the book.

**The Belief That Precedes the Submitting**

The fact that the belief of the disciples precedes, in the order of the verse, their submitting to God in the state of the full submission, indicates, in the reading that is kept in the state of the theology, that the belief that is in the inside should precede the conforming of the actions of the one to the will of God in the state of the outside, and the indication is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the showing of the order of the two states. The idea is reflected in the formulation of the tradition of the Islam that keeps the faith in the state of the knowledge in the heart and the affirmation with the tongue and the proper action with the limbs, and the naming of the formulation is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the expression of the order of the belief and the submission in the account of the disciples.

**The Verse in the Account of the Sūrah**

The present verse stands in the account of the sūrah between the address of God to Jesus in the previous verse and the question of the disciples in the verse that follows, and the position of the verse is the reading that keeps the account of the sūrah in the state of the sequence of the inspiring of the disciples and the testimony of the disciples and the request of the table. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the keeping of the testimony of the disciples before the request of the table, and the keeping is the reading that shows the state of the belief of the disciples in the time before the asking of the sign, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the state of the faith that is in the disciples before the request of the confirmation.**The Account of the Table in the Sūrah**

The account of the sūrah that keeps the incident of the table runs from the inspiring of the disciples in the present verse to the question of the disciples, to the answer of the disciples, and to the prayer of Jesus, and the running of the account in this order is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the sequence of the events of the table. The table is the thing from which the sūrah takes its name, and the naming of the sūrah by the table is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the title, and the title of the sūrah keeps the account of the table in the state of the central event of the last part of the sūrah. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the opening of the account of the table in the state of the inspiring of the disciples, and the opening is the reading that shows the state of the belief of the disciples before the request of the table, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the condition of the disciples in the time before the asking of the sign from God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:112

> ˹Remember˺ when the disciples asked, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Would your Lord be willing to send down to us a table spread with food from heaven?” Jesus answered, “Fear Allah if you are ˹truly˺ believers.”

**The Question of the Disciples**

The disciples, in the question that they keep in the verse, ask Jesus whether the Lord of Jesus is the able to send down to them from the heaven the table that is spread with the food, and the question is, in the state of the account, the one that suggests a certain doubt in the omnipotence of God, since the disciples ask whether God is the able to do the thing. The disciples may have desired such food for the simple nourishment of the body, or for the spiritual blessing of the consuming of the food of the heaven, or for both of the two together, and the naming of the two desires is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the desire of the disciples.

**The Answer of Jesus in the State of the Reverence**

It is the apparent disrespect, or the lack of the appreciation of the power of God, that the question suggests, that leads Jesus to the answer in the state of the calling of the disciples to the reverence of God in the condition of their being the believers, and the answer is kept in the tone of the rebuke, according to some of the commentators. The expression of the reverence of God also carries the meaning of the having of the fear of God, and the taking of the expression in this meaning is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the expression. The request of the disciples comes, in the order of the sūrah, shortly after the recounting of the many of the miraculous abilities of Jesus in the previous verse, and the request may suggest a certain ingratitude for the signs that the disciples have already been given, and the naming of the ingratitude is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the state of the request.

**The Other Reading of the Question**

Some of the early commentators read the verse in the indication that the disciples were asking Jesus whether he was the able to beseech God on their behalf for the sending down of the table, and not in the questioning of the ability of God to do the thing, and the reading is the form that the commentator does in the state of the distinction of the two questions. In the latter case, the answer of Jesus is kept in the state of the rebuke, since the asking of the sign may indicate the lack of the faith or the certainty in the status of Jesus as the prophet, and the state of the asking of the sign is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the questioning of the state of the faith of the disciples, despite their claim of the faith in the previous verse. The sūrah contains several other accounts of the people who request the sign from their prophets on the ground of the lack of the faith, and the similar statements are kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that speaks of the demanding of the revelation in the writing from the heaven, in the verse of the sūrah al-Anʿām that asks why no sign has been sent down from the Lord, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Anʿām that keeps the statement that the signs are only with God and that the one who swears by God that he would believe if a sign came is the one who is questioned about the believing.

**The Verse in the Account of the Table**

The present verse stands in the account of the sūrah as the verse of the request of the table, and the verse that follows keeps the answer of the disciples to the answer of Jesus, and the two verses together keep the account of the request in the state of the question and the answer. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the keeping of the question of the disciples before the answer of Jesus, and the keeping is the reading that shows the state of the asking of the sign in the time before the request of the table from God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the sequence of the request and the answer in the state of the table.

**The Request of the Sign in the Context of the Sūrah**

The request of the disciples comes, in the order of the account of the sūrah, after the address of God to Jesus in the state of the recollection of the favor, and after the inspiring of the disciples and the testimony of the disciples in the state of the belief, and the coming of the request in this place is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the keeping of the request after the two states of the recollection and of the testimony. The sūrah keeps the request of the table in the state of the asking of the confirmation of the truth of the message of Jesus, and the keeping is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the faith of the disciples in the time of the asking. The similar requests of the sign are kept in the sūrah in the state of the questioning of the people who ask the sign from their prophets, and the naming of the similar requests is the reading that keeps the request of the disciples in the state of the parallel of the other requests of the sign, and the parallel is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the state of the asking of the confirmation in the time before the giving of the confirmation by God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:113

> They said, “We ˹only˺ wish to eat from it to reassure our hearts, to verify you are indeed truthful to us, and to become its witnesses.”

**The Desire of the Reassurance of the Hearts**

The answer of the disciples, in the verse, expresses the desire that the hearts of the disciples may be in the state of the reassurance, and that the disciples may have the direct confirmation of the message of the prophethy of Jesus, and the desire of the reassurance of the hearts is the reading that the sūrah does in the mouth of the disciples after the answer of Jesus. The motivation of the disciples is, in the reading of the commentators, similar to the motivation of Abraham in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow in which Abraham says to his Lord that He should show him the giving of the life to the dead, and the answer of God is the question of the believing of Abraham, and the answer of Abraham is the statement that he believes, but that the heart should be in the state of the reassurance, and the naming of the verse of Abraham is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the parallel of the two motivations in the state of the asking of the reassurance of the heart.

**The Reminder Rather Than the Rebuke**

The words of Jesus, in which he calls the disciples to the reverence of God in the condition of their being the believers, may not be kept in the state of the rebuke at all, but in the state of the reminder that the attitude of the reverence would lead God to the fulfilling of the request of the disciples, and the taking of the words as the reminder is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the softening of the sense of the answer of Jesus. The sūrah, in the present verse, does not keep the determination of the state of the answer of Jesus in the form of the rebuke or the reminder, and the keeping of the two readings is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the leaving of the sense in the state of the openness, and the leaving of the sense is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the answer of Jesus to the question of the disciples.

**The Witness to Which the Disciples Promise**

The promise of the disciples to be the witnesses to the table means, in the reading of some of the commentators, that the disciples will be the witnesses to the miracle of the table for the ones who are not present at it, and the reading is the form that the commentator does in the state of the witnessing to the event of the table. The other reading is that the disciples will be the witnesses to the truth of the oneness of God and of the power of God, and to the prophethy of Jesus, and the taking of the promise in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the extension of the witnessing from the state of the event to the state of the truth of the religion. The sūrah, in the present verse, keeps the promise of the witnesses in the state of the two readings, and the keeping is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the leaving of the sense of the promise in the state of the openness between the witnessing to the event and the witnessing to the truth.

**The Parallel with the Feast of the Breaking of the Fast**

According to one of the commentators, the request of the disciples followed the fast of the thirty days, and Jesus had, in the time after the fast, advised the disciples that God would grant them any need that they might have, and the advising creates the parallel between this account and the feast of the Islam that breaks the fast that follows the month of the fasting, and the verse that follows indicates, in the request of Jesus to God, that the table should be the feast for the disciples, and the naming of the feast is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the keeping of the parallel between the two accounts of the feast. The story of the table has no direct parallel in the accounts of the Gospels, and some of the commentators consider it the thing that is known only to the people of the Islam through the sūrah. The story may be linked with the account of the Gospels of the multiplying of the loaves and the fishes, or more indirectly with the story of the last supper, and by the extension to the food of the heaven of the Eucharist of the Christians that is based on it. In the state of the story of the faith and of the miracles, the story may also be compared with the accounts of the Gospels in which Jesus rebukes the disciples for the lacking of the faith in the ability of God to provide the food for them, or in which the lacking of the faith is kept as the thing that prevents the miraculous intervention of God.

**The Answer of the Disciples to the Answer of Jesus**

The present verse keeps the answer of the disciples to the answer of Jesus, and the answer of the disciples is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the continuation of the dialogue of the two, and the continuation of the dialogue is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the request of the table. The disciples, in their answer, do not take the words of Jesus in the state of the rejection of the request, and the not taking of the words in the state of the rejection is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the perseverance of the disciples in the request of the table, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the condition of the disciples in the time between the answer of Jesus and the prayer of Jesus to God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:114

> Jesus, son of Mary, prayed, “O Allah, our Lord! Send us from heaven a table spread with food as a feast for us—the first and last of us—and as a sign from You. Provide for us! You are indeed the Best Provider.”

**The Prayer of Jesus to God**

Jesus, in the present verse, keeps the prayer to God in the form of the calling of God as the Lord, and the prayer is the asking of the sending down of the table from the heaven that is spread with the food, and the prayer is the act that Jesus does in the state of the request of the sign after the account of the question of the disciples and the answer of the disciples in the previous verses. The sūrah, in the present verse, keeps the prayer in the mouth of Jesus, and the keeping is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the asking of the sign in the way of the prayer rather than the way of the demand, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the humility of Jesus before God in the time of the asking.

**The Feast for the First and the Last**

The prayer of Jesus keeps, in the first of its requests, the asking of the table as the feast for the disciples, and the feast is kept in the state of the feast for the first of the disciples and the last of the disciples, and the keeping of the feast in this state is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the extension of the blessing of the table from the state of the time of the disciples to the state of the times that come after. The first and the last of the disciples is the reading that keeps the table in the state of the sign for the community of the disciples in all of its times, and the naming of the feast in the word that the sūrah keeps is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the connection of the table with the feast of the breaking of the fast that the sūrah keeps in the account of the request of the disciples.

**The Sign Requested from God**

The prayer of Jesus keeps, in the second of its requests, the asking of the table as the sign from God, and the sign is the form that the table takes in the state of the showing of the power of God to the people, and the asking of the sign is the act that Jesus does in the form of the request of the confirmation of the truth of the message of his prophethy. The state of the sign is the reading that the sūrah does in the connection of the table with the truth of the prophethy of Jesus, and the connection is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the table as the confirmation of the message. The similar asking of the sign is kept in the sūrah in the state of the prayer of the prophets to their Lord, and the verse of the sūrah al-Anbiyāʾ that keeps the account of the one who cried out in the state of the darkness and the answering of God to his prayer is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the parallel of the prayer of Jesus and the prayer of the one who is in the state of the need before God.

**The Providing of the Best Provider**

The prayer of Jesus keeps, in the third of its requests, the asking of the providing from God, and the asking is kept in the state of the calling of God as the best of the providers, and the calling of God in this name is the reading that Jesus does in the form of the putting of the request in the hand of the one who is the best of the providers. The sūrah, in the prayer, does in the form of the keeping of the request of the providing after the request of the feast and the request of the sign, and the keeping is the reading that shows the state of the order of the requests in the prayer of Jesus, and the order of the requests is the form that the prayer takes in the account of the sūrah. The three requests of the prayer, in the state of the feast and the sign and the providing, keep the account of the table in the form of the showing of the purpose of the table for the disciples, and the showing of the purpose is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the connection of the table with the needs of the body and with the needs of the confirmation and with the needs of the provision, and the connection is the form that the prayer of Jesus does in the account of the sūrah.

**The Verse in the Account of the Table**

The present verse stands in the account of the sūrah as the verse of the prayer of Jesus, and the verses that follow keep the account of the sending down of the table by God and the warning of God to the one who denies after the sending down, and the two parts together keep the account of the table in the state of the prayer and the answering. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the keeping of the prayer of Jesus before the sending down of the table, and the keeping is the reading that shows the state of the request of the sign in the time before the giving of the sign by God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the sequence of the prayer and the answering in the state of the table.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:115

> Allah answered, “I am sending it down to you. But whoever among you denies afterwards will be subjected to a torment I have never inflicted on anyone of My creation.”

**The Answer of God to the Prayer of Jesus**

God, in the present verse, keeps the answer to the prayer of Jesus in the state of the declaration of the sending down of the table, and the answer is the form that God does in the state of the fulfilling of the promise to the one who has asked. The declaration of the sending down is the reading that the sūrah does in the mouth of God after the prayer of Jesus in the previous verse, and the keeping of the answer in the state of the declaration is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the answering of the prayer in the way of the promise rather than the way of the postponement. The sūrah, in the present verse, keeps the principle that the promise of God is the one that comes to pass in the state of the declaration of the sending down, and the keeping of the principle is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the fulfillment of the promise of God to the one who has asked in the state of the prayer.

**The Feast of the Table for the First and the Last**

The prayer of Jesus, in the previous verse, kept the asking of the table as the feast for the disciples, and the feast is the word that the sūrah keeps in the form of the meaning of the returning and the joy, and the word is the one that is used in the state of the denoting of the holidays of the religion of the Islam. The prayer of Jesus may simply be the request of the sending down of the sufficient food for all of the followers of Jesus, and the taking of the prayer in this reading is the form that the commentators do in the state of the explanation of the asking of the food. The wish of Jesus, in the other reading, is the indication of the state in which he and his followers, in the state of the present and the future, would keep the day of the event as the feast of the religion, and the taking of the wish in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the extension of the sense of the feast from the state of the food to the state of the day of the feast. Some of the commentators say that this feast, which is kept in the state of the perpetual observing, is the day of the Sunday, and the naming of the day of the Sunday is the reading that supports the suggestion that the account of the sūrah is the reference to the Eucharist of the Christians.

**The Accounts of the Food that Came Down**

The various reports that the commentators keep for the food of the table indicate that the food was the fish, or the meat and the bread, or the fruits of the paradise, or all of the kinds of the food except the meat, and the keeping of the various reports is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the leaving of the determination of the kind of the food in the state of the openness. According to one of the reports, the table came down in the state of the red cloth that was spread with the food, between the two clouds, and Jesus prayed in the time of the coming down, asking God to make him in the state of the thankful, and to make the table in the state of the mercy and not in the state of the punishment, and the naming of the prayer of Jesus is the reading that the report does in the form of the keeping of the state of the humility of Jesus in the time of the miracle. The keeping of the various accounts of the commentators is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the food of the table in the state of the accounts of the people of the commentary.

**The Report of the Not Sending Down**

Some of the commentators keep the maintenance that no table that was spread with the food was sent down at all, and that the story of the sūrah is simply the parable that was told to the people in the state of the discouraging of the asking of such things in the time after, and the taking of the story in this reading is the form that the commentator does in the state of the explanation of the purpose of the story. Some of the reports keep the statement that the disciples withdrew the request of the table and took the repentance in the time after the hearing of the warning of God in the present verse, and the warning is the statement of the punishment of the one who disbelieves in the time after the sending down, in the state of the torment that has not been kept for any of the creation of God. However, the affirmation of God, in the present verse, that the table is to be sent down, and the principle that the promise of God is the one that comes to pass, keep, in the reading of most of the commentators, the state of the actual sending down of the table, and the taking of the table in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the following of the affirmation of God over the state of the parable.

**The Table as the Descent of the Spiritual Blessing**

Despite the various accounts of the commentators of the contents of the table, the table may represent, in the state of the account, above all, the descent of the blessings of the spirit and the nourishment of the spirit, and the representation of the table in this state is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the extension of the sense of the table from the state of the food of the body to the state of the food of the spirit. The sūrah, in the account of the table, does in the form of the keeping of the two states of the table in the state of the openness, and the keeping of the two states is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the sense of the table for the body and for the spirit of the people of the faith. The present verse stands in the account of the sūrah as the verse of the answer of God, and the position of the verse is the reading that keeps the account of the table in the state of the sequence of the prayer of Jesus and the answer of God and the warning of God to the one who denies after the sending down, and the keeping of the sequence is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the events of the table.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:116

> And ˹on Judgment Day˺ Allah will say, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Did you ever ask the people to worship you and your mother as gods besides Allah?” He will answer, “Glory be to You! How could I ever say what I had no right to say? If I had said such a thing, you would have certainly known it. You know what is ˹hidden˺ within me, but I do not know what is within You. Indeed, You ˹alone˺ are the Knower of all unseen.

**The Time of the Questioning of Jesus**

Some of the commentators assert that the questioning of Jesus in the present verse took place in the time immediately after the raising up of Jesus, and the similar statement of the raising up is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that says that God will take Jesus and raise him up to Himself and deliver him from the ones who disbelieve, and in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that says that God raised Jesus up to Himself and that God is the almighty and the wise. The present verse, in the reading that is kept, brings the account of the sūrah back to the question that God keeps to the prophets, which the sūrah holds in the state of the day of the judgment, and the bringing back of the account is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the continuation of the account of the day that began in the verse of the sūrah that keeps the gathering of the messengers and the question of God to them.

**The Exaggeration of the Spiritual Station of Jesus**

Although many of the prophets and the messages of the prophets passed from the memory of the people in the time, the matter of the present verse is the state in which the spiritual station of Jesus was exaggerated by his followers in the time after his passing to the point at which they attributed the divinity to him and to his mother, and the naming of the exaggeration is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the account of the attribution of the divinity. The present verse follows, in the order of the sūrah, immediately after the warning of the previous verse that the one who disbelieves in the time after the miracle is the one who is in the state of the punished, and the warning is the one that the sūrah keeps after the account of the table, and the following of the verse is the reading that suggests that the disbelief of the attribution of the divinity is the disbelief of which the disciples had been warned in the previous verse, and the suggestion is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the connection of the two verses.

**The Doctrine of Mary and the Criticism of the Divinization**

The sūrah, in the present verse, may be referring to the doctrines of the Christians regarding Mary, in which she is identified as the mother of God, and the doctrine is the extension of the belief of the Christians in the divinity of the Christ, and the naming of the doctrine is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the criticism of the attribution of the divinity to Mary. The criticism of the sūrah of the attribution of the divinity to Mary may also do in the form of the suggestion that she is taken in the state of the third person of the trinity that the sūrah criticizes, as the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the statement of the saying that God is one in the trinity keeps the similar statement, and as the verse of the present sūrah that speaks of the saying of the oneness of God in the state of the trinity keeps the similar statement in the form of the declaration of the disbelief. The criticism is not kept in the state of the holy spirit, since the holy spirit is used in the sūrah, as the verse of the sūrah has said, primarily in the state of the name of the archangel Gabriel, and the sūrah does not suggest that the archangel was attributed the divinity by the people. The sūrah, in the present verse, may be criticizing, rather than the doctrines of the Christians concerning Mary, the exaggerations of the Christians of the common people of the status of Mary that approach the attribution of the divinity, and the similar criticism is kept in the verse of the sūrah at-Tawbah that says that the people have taken their rabbis and their monks as the lords apart from God, and as well as the son of Mary who is the Messiah, although the people were commanded to worship the one God.

**The Miracles and the Consideration of the Divinity**

In the connection of the belief in the state of the divinity of Jesus with the miraculous actions of Jesus that the verse of the sūrah that rehearses the favor of God upon Jesus recounted, the commentator who is named in the account says that, if the Christians consider the miracles of Jesus as the things that he made of his own will, in the sense that the full control over the miracles was in the hand of him and not in the hand of God, they consider him, in the state of the account, in the position of the divinity of God, and the naming of this reading is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the connection of the miracles with the belief of the divinity. Jesus, in the answer that he keeps in the verse, shows that the bearing of the responsibility for the exaggerations of his status or the status of the mother of him is not upon him, and the showing of the not bearing of the responsibility is the reading that the sūrah does in the mouth of Jesus in the state of the putting of the exaggerations apart from the command of Jesus.

**The Response of the Perfect Humility**

Rather than denying directly that he commanded the followers to attribute the divinity to him and to the mother of him apart from God, Jesus keeps the attitude of the proper deportment before God in the form of the offering of the response of the perfect humility, and the response is the statement that the uttering of such a thing was not from the right of him, and the taking of the response in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the showing of the humility of Jesus before God. The answer of Jesus that follows is in the state of the parallel of the response that the prophets gave in the verse of the sūrah that keeps the gathering of the messengers, and the parallel is the reading that indicates that the testimony of Jesus is ultimately not necessary, since God knows all of the things, including the things of the unseen, and Jesus properly defers the matter to God, and the deferring is the act that Jesus does in the form of the putting of the matter in the hand of God.

**The Knowledge of the Self and the Essence**

The statement of Jesus that the things which are in his self are in the state of the known to God, and that the things which are in the Self of God are not in the state of the known to him, may simply mean, in the reading of some of the commentators, that the knowledge of Jesus is in the state of the known to God, while the knowledge of God remains in the state of the unknown to Jesus, and the taking of the statement in this reading is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the knowledge. The other reading is that the things that Jesus might keep hidden within himself are in the state of the known to God, while the things that are hidden in God are not in the state of the known to Jesus, and the taking of the statement in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the hiding. The word of the self, which is used in connection with both Jesus and God, is the translation of the word that, in the case of Jesus, refers to his human soul, and that, in the case of God, is taken in the meaning of the essence of God, and the naming of the two meanings of the word is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the distinction of the two uses of the word in the account of the verse.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:117

> I never told them anything except what You ordered me to say: “Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord!” And I was witness over them as long as I remained among them. But when You took me, You were the Witness over them —and You are a Witness over all things.

**The Command That Jesus Kept**

Jesus, in the present verse, keeps the statement that he told the people nothing except what God ordered him to say, and the command that he kept is the command of the worship of God as the Lord of Jesus and the Lord of the people, and the keeping of the command in this form is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the declaration of the content of the message of Jesus. The command of the worship of God as the Lord of the one and the Lord of the people is the formulation of the prophetic message that is kept throughout the sūrah, and Jesus gives the same command in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that keeps the declaration that God is the Lord of Jesus and the Lord of the people and that this is the straight path, and in the verse of the present sūrah that speaks of the saying of the Messiah to the Children of Israel that they should worship God as the Lord of the Messiah and the Lord of the people, and the similar command is kept in the verse of the sūrah Meryam, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Zukhruf that keeps the similar declaration in the mouth of Jesus.

**The Command in the Mouths of the Other Prophets**

The formulation of the command is attributed to Hud in the verse of the sūrah Hūd that keeps the statement of the putting of the trust in God as the Lord of the one and the Lord of the people, and to Moses in the verse of the sūrah Ghāfir that keeps the seeking of the refuge in the Lord of the one and the Lord of the people from the one who is arrogant, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Dukhān that keeps the similar seeking of the refuge with the Lord of the one and the Lord of the people, and to Muhammad in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the statement of the Lord of the one and the Lord of the people in the state of the dispute about God, and in the verse of the sūrah ash-Shūrā that keeps the similar statement in the state of the command of the inviting to the religion and the judging among the people with the fairness, and the naming of the verses of the prophets is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the commonness of the formulation of the prophetic message in the mouths of the prophets from the first to the last.

**The Witness of Jesus in the Time of the Remaining**

Jesus, in the present verse, keeps the statement that he was the witness over the people in the time so long as he remained among them, and the witness of Jesus is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the limitation of the witnessing of Jesus to the duration of his earthly life. Continuing in the same tone of the humility that the sūrah keeps in the verses of the account of the messengers, Jesus claims no ability to be the witness for his people in the time after his time on the earth, and the claiming of the no ability is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the keeping of the witness of Jesus in the state of the time of the remaining among the people. God, in the statement of the verse, is the witness over the people in the time after the taking of Jesus, and God is the witness over all of the things, and the naming of the witnessing of God is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the extension of the witnessing from the state of the time of Jesus to the state of all of the times and all of the things.

**The Taking of Jesus by God**

The expression of the verse that God took Jesus is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the reference to the raising up of Jesus, and the similar statement of the raising up is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that says that God will take Jesus and raise him up to Himself, and in the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that says that God raised Jesus up to Himself, and the taking of the expression in this reading is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the connection of the statement of the verse with the account of the raising up of Jesus. The sūrah does not keep, in the present verse, the determination of the manner of the taking of Jesus in the form of the statement, and the keeping of the manner in the state of the account of the raising up is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the expression of the taking in the state of the raising up rather than in the state of the taking to the death.

**The Purpose of the Question of God**

Some of the commentators maintain that the purpose of the question of God to Jesus in the previous verse was the informing of Jesus of the actions of his followers in the time after he was no longer among them, and not the obtaining of the knowledge of the things that God, in the state of the omniscience, would have already known, and the taking of the question in this reading is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the explanation of the purpose of the question. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the keeping of the statement of Jesus in the state of the answer to the question, and the keeping is the reading that shows the state of the informing of Jesus of the actions of his followers in the time after the taking of Jesus by God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the answer of Jesus to the question of God.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:118

> If You punish them, they belong to You after all. But if You forgive them, You are surely the Almighty, All-Wise.”

**The Not Suggesting of the Punishment or the Forgiveness**

Jesus, in the present verse, manifests further the humility before God in the form of the refusing of the suggesting of either the punishment of the followers who have erred or the forgiveness of the followers who have erred, and he puts the matter in the hand of God in the state of the fullness, and the putting of the matter in the hand of God is the reading that the sūrah does in the mouth of Jesus in the state of the leaving of the decision to God. The two states of the punishment and the forgiveness are kept in the verse in the form of the two conditions, and the keeping of the two conditions is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the leaving of the decision in the hand of God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the response of Jesus to the question of God.

**The Servants of God in the State of the Two Conditions**

The first condition of the verse, in which Jesus says that the followers belong to God in the state of the punishing, is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the declaration of the belonging of the followers to God in all of the states, and the declaration of the belonging is the reading that Jesus does in the state of the acknowledgment of the ownership of God over the followers in the time before the decision and in the time after the decision. The second condition of the verse, in which Jesus calls God the almighty and the wise in the state of the forgiving, is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the putting of the forgiving in the state of the power and the wisdom of God, and the putting of the forgiving in this state is the reading that Jesus does in the form of the showing of the state of the forgiving as the act of the one who is the almighty and the wise, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the two conditions of the verse.

**The Error of the Attribution of the Divinity and the Shirk**

The error of the taking of Jesus and his mother as the gods apart from God, which the previous verse kept in the state of the question of God to Jesus, may be considered by some in the form of the shirk, which is the ascribing of the partners to God, and the shirk is the one that is not forgiven, according to the statement of the sūrah of the Cow in the two verses that keep the declaration of the not forgiving of the ascribing of the partners to God and the forgiving of whatever is other than it for whom God wills. Many of the commentators, however, suggest that these beliefs of the Christians do not actually rise to the level of the shirk, but they are considered in the state of the sins that are the mere, and God may forgive these sins in the state of the account, and the taking of the beliefs in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the distinction of the shirk from the sins that are forgiven by God.

**The Freedom of God in the Forgiving**

The school of the theologians who refuse to limit the freedom of God in the assigning of the reward or the punishment by making it contingent upon the actions of the people considers the possible forgiveness of God of the errors of the belief of the Christians as the thing that is in the state of the fullness within the scope of the action of His justice and of His wisdom, and the consideration of the forgiveness in this reading is the reading that the school does in the form of the keeping of the freedom of God in the state of the action of the justice and the wisdom. The sūrah, in the present verse, keeps the suggestion of Jesus that God may forgive the followers in the state of the leaving of the matter completely in the hand of God, and the keeping of the suggestion is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the possibility of the forgiveness in the hand of God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the suggestion of Jesus.

**The Intercession of the Prophets for their People**

The suggestion of Jesus that God may forgive the followers, in the state of the leaving of the matter completely in the hands of God, shows the manner that is proper in the state of the interceding of the prophets for their people, and some of the commentators consider the present verse as the proof of the accepting by God of the intercession of the prophets for the followers who have sinned, and the naming of the verse as the proof is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the showing of the intercession of Jesus for his people in the state of the humility before God. According to the hadith that is kept in the account of the commentators, the prophet was once heard in the state of the praying from the night until the morning, and he recited only the present verse in each of the cycles of the prayer, and when the companion who is named in the account made the question of this matter to him, the answer of the prophet was the statement that the asking of his Lord for the gift of the intercession for his community was the act that he did, and that the gift was the given to all of the ones who were innocent of the sin of the shirk, and the naming of the hadith is the reading that the account of the commentators does in the form of the connection of the intercession of Jesus in the present verse with the intercession of the prophet for his community in the state of the asking of the Lord.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:119

> Allah will declare, “This is the Day when ˹only˺ the faithful will benefit from their faithfulness. Theirs are Gardens under which rivers flow, to stay there for ever and ever. Allah is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him. That is the ultimate triumph.”

**The Final Part of the Address of God to Jesus**

The commentators consider the statement of God in the present verse as the part of the address of God to Jesus that is the last, which the commentators take in the time immediately after the raising up of Jesus, or, in the reading that is more likely, in the time of the day of the judgment, and the taking of the statement in this reading is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the time of the address. The present verse concludes the account of the reckoning of God with the messengers and the followers of the messengers, which the sūrah keeps in the verses of the account of the messengers, with the promise of the bliss of the hereafter for the ones who are truthful, and the concluding of the account with the promise of the bliss is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the closing of the account of the reckoning in the state of the reward of the hereafter.

**The Reward of the Truthfulness**

The similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah al-Aḥzāb that keeps the covenant that God established with the prophets and the questioning of the men of the truth concerning their truthfulness, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Aḥzāb that keeps the rewarding of God of the faithful for their faithfulness, and the naming of the verses is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the connection of the present verse with the account of the truthfulness of the prophets in the sūrah of the sūrah al-Aḥzāb. The reward of the truthfulness is, in the state of the account, kept for the ones who had the truthfulness in the state of the lives of the earth, in the time in which the truthfulness was the matter of the choice, and the limiting of the reward to the time of the choice is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the explanation of the reward. The one cannot be in the state of the not being truthful in the day of the judgment, and not even Satan is in the state of the avoiding of the truthfulness, and they are not in the state of the rewarded for this truthfulness, and the not being rewarded of the truthfulness of the day is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the distinction of the truthfulness of the choice from the truthfulness of the compulsion of the day.

**The Gardens with the Rivers and the Mutual Contentment**

The present verse brings together the several common descriptions of the bliss of the paradise that are repeated throughout the sūrah, and the bringing together of the descriptions is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the gathering of the images of the bliss. The ones who are blessed abide forever in the gardens under which the rivers flow, and the image is kept dozens of times throughout the sūrah, as the verse of the sūrah of the Cow that keeps the giving of the good news of the gardens under which the rivers flow to the ones who believe and do good keeps the similar statement. They experience the state of the mutual contentment with God, and the similar statement is kept in the verse of the sūrah at-Tawbah that keeps the statement that God is pleased with the foremost and they are pleased with Him, in the verse of the sūrah al-Mujādilah that keeps the similar statement in the account of the party of God, in the verse of the sūrah al-Bayyinah, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Fajr that keeps the statement of the returning to the Lord in the state of the being well pleased and well pleasing. The commentator who is named in the account describes this state of the mutual contentment as one of the secrets of the paradise that are the wondrous, and whose reality is not in the state of the described in the words, and the naming of the state as the wondrous secret is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the showing of the state of the contentment that is beyond the description.

**The Contentment as the Characteristic of the Paradise**

The bliss of the hereafter is, in the state of the account of the sūrah, always kept in the form of the joy that is in the state of the calm and the peace, and the contentment is kept in the state of the characteristic of the existence of the paradise in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that keeps the good news of the gardens with the pleasure of God for the ones who are mindful of God, in the verse of the sūrah at-Tawbah that keeps the good news of the mercy and the pleasure and the gardens for the ones who believe, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Hajj that keeps the statement of the admitting of the people into the place with which they are pleased. The deeds are considered in the state of the righteousness in the state of the being undertaken with the intention of the seeking of the contentment of God with them, and the naming of the intention is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the connection of the deeds with the contentment of God. The attaining of the reward of the paradise is described as the ultimate triumph, and the phrase is connected with the blessings of the paradise throughout the sūrah, as the verse of the sūrah al-Anfāl that keeps the statement of the admittance of the believing men and women into the gardens and the ultimate triumph keeps the similar statement, and as the verse of the sūrah al-Jāthiyah that keeps the similar statement in the account of the admitting of the ones who believe and do good into the mercy keeps the similar statement.

**The Ultimate Triumph as the Mutual Contentment**

Some of the commentators indicate that, in the present verse, the ultimate triumph is described in the state of the mutual contentment with God, and the other blessings of the paradise are not in the state of the included in the description, and the description of the ultimate triumph in this state is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the limiting of the triumph to the contentment. This is supported by the reading of the present verse in connection with the verse of the sūrah at-Tawbah that says that God has kept the promise to the men who believe and the women who believe of the gardens under which the rivers flow, and the good dwellings in the gardens of the eternity, and that the pleasure of God is above all, and that it is the ultimate triumph, and the reading of the two verses together is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the connection of the two statements of the triumph. The contentment of God is, in the reading of the commentators, taken in the form of the allusion to the highest of the paradise, and the alluding of the contentment to the highest of the paradise is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the showing of the state of the contentment as the highest of the blessings of the paradise.

---

## Sūrah al-Māʾidah 5:120

> To Allah ˹alone˺ belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and everything within. And He is Most Capable of everything.

**The Final Statement of the Sūrah**

The present verse is the final verse of the sūrah, and it keeps the statement that to God belongs the kingdom that is over the heavens and over the earth and over all of the things that are within them, and that He is the powerful over all of the things, and the keeping of the statement in this position is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the closing of the sūrah in the state of the sovereignty of God. Some of the commentators view this statement as the final rebuke to the ones who would attribute the divinity to Jesus and Mary, which the verse of the sūrah that keeps the questioning of Jesus mentioned, and the naming of the statement as the rebuke is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the showing of the state of the falling under the sovereignty of God and the power of God of the ones who attribute the divinity to the ones who are under the sovereignty of God.

**The Warning of the Power of God over All of the Creation**

The commentator who is named in the account says that the present verse is meant as the final warning to the people that they, like all of the created beings, in the state of the animate and the inanimate, are in the state of the being under the power of God in the state of the fullness, and in the state of the submitted to the will of God, and the warning is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the showing of the state of the submission of the creation to the will of God. The commentator also says that whatever knowledge or power the people may take upon themselves is, in the state of the account, nothing before the knowledge of God and the power of God, and the naming of the taking upon themselves of the knowledge and the power is the reading that the commentator does in the form of the showing of the state of the nothingness of the knowledge and the power of the people before the knowledge and the power of God.

**The Opening and the Closing of the Sūrah**

The commentator who is named in the account notes that the sūrah opens with the reminder to the believers to honour their obligations to God, and the sūrah proceeds to outline the various requirements of the ritual and the law that the believers must meet, and the outlining of the requirements is the reading that the sūrah does in the state of the account of the requirements of the religion. But the sūrah ends in the state of the absorption of the individual soul that is the final, and the soul is in the state of the absorbed in the totality of the reality of God, and the ending of the sūrah in this state is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the absorption of the soul in the reality of God, and the showing is the form that the sūrah does in the account of the closing of the sūrah.

**The Kingdom of God in the Sūrah**

The similar statement of the belonging of the kingdom of the heavens and the earth to God is kept in the verse of the sūrah of the Family that says that to God belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and that God is the powerful over all of the things, and in the verse of the sūrah al-Ḥadīd that says that to Him belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and that He gives life and causes death, and the naming of the similar statements is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the commonness of the statement of the kingdom of God in the sūrah. The present verse keeps the statement of the kingdom of God in the state of the closing of the sūrah, and the keeping of the statement in this position is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the state of the sovereignty of God as the final word of the sūrah.

**The Servanthood and the Lordship in the Sūrah**

If the sūrah opens in the state of the putting down of the laws that govern the servant, and in the state of the relationship of the servanthood to the lordship, it ends in the state of the reminder that all of that which is other than God is in the state of the contingency of the essence, and that it is in the state of the remaining completely within the sovereignty of God and the power of God, and the ending of the sūrah in this reminder is the reading that the commentators do in the form of the showing of the state of the contingency of the creation before the sovereignty of God. The sūrah, in the present verse, does in the form of the closing of the account of the sūrah in the state of the sovereignty of God that is over the heavens and over the earth and over all of the things, and the closing is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the keeping of the sūrah in the state of the sovereignty of God from the first verse to the last verse, and the keeping of the sūrah in this state is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the sovereignty of God as the frame of the sūrah.

**The Verse as the Closing Word of the Sūrah**

The present verse is the word of the sūrah that closes the account of the sūrah, and the verse does in the form of the putting of the sovereignty of God as the last statement of the sūrah after the account of the questioning of Jesus and the account of the reward of the truthful and the account of the intercession of Jesus for his people. The sūrah, in the present verse, leaves the reader in the state of the sovereignty of God over the heavens and the earth and over all of the things, and the leaving of the reader in this state is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the showing of the sovereignty of God as the frame of the sūrah and as the final word of the sūrah. The keeping of the statement of the sovereignty in the last place of the sūrah is the reading that the sūrah does in the form of the closing of the sūrah in the state of the sovereignty of God, and the closing is the reading that the sūrah does in the account of the sūrah.

---
